Introduction
Operation of the aircraft without certain
secondary airframe and engine parts is possible as indicated in this
Master Configuration Deviation List. Any part not included in this
list must be considered as necessary.  It is important to repair the aircraft
at the first airport where repairs or replacements may reasonably
be made, since additional malfunctions may require the airplane to
be taken out of service.  Letter
(m) associated to an item indicates that maintenance action is necessary
to permit flight with these parts missing.
It is the Operator's responsibility
to:
Define the task
sharing between the flight and maintenance crews
Ensure that
all maintenance procedures are performed.
When items require high-speed
tape, the MCDL dispatch conditions are assessed considering that the
tape is in the correct position and in good condition. The Operator
must use an appropriate process to ensure that the dispatch conditions
are satisfied. The Operator must define this process in accordance
with its specific operations.
Unless otherwise specified in
the MCDL item or the relevant AMM task, a visual inspection of the
high-speed tape must be made at a regular interval that must not exceed
3 calendar days.
The  MCDL  maintenance procedures
are published in the  AMM . The  MCDL  item number
may be used to find the associated task in the  AMM  via
the dedicated function of AirN@v.
The sign “-”
in “Quantity installed” column indicates that the quantity is variable.
The illustrations
included in this Master Configuration Deviation List are given only
for information to facilitate location of missing items and must not
be considered as approved data.
Items numbering
is used for item identification only. As a consequence it may appear
some gaps in the item numbering sequence of a given aircraft. In such
a case, completeness of the MCDL may be checked by referring to the  LEDU .
Limitations
No more than one part of one
system may be missing except if otherwise specified. Parts of different
systems may be simultaneously missing, unless otherwise specified
in this list.  When missing
part introduces additional limitation(s), this limitation is indicated
in the dispatch condition of the item of this list. This limitation
comes in addition to the ones of the LIMITATIONS chapter of this  AFM . This limitation must be clearly indicated by a placard
on the pilote's instrument panel.  When an  MCDL  dispatch condition refers to the  MMEL , the minimum number of equipment required for dispatch is the most
limiting of the two documents.
Performance Determination Method
There are two approved ways of determining
the performance impact of  CDL  items missing:
Using  MCDL  chapter of the  AFM , or
Using AFM_OCTO
software.
The  MCDL  performance penalties associated to the missing items
when published in this chapter are envelope penalties. More accurate
penalties can be determined by using AFM_OCTO software. According
to the operations, operators can select the most adequate method.  If no performance data are available
in AFM_OCTO for a given item listed in this  MCDL  chapter, the penalties published in this  MCDL  chapter
must be used.
Performance Penalties published in the Airplane Flight Manual
MCDL Chapter
Performance penalties are cumulative
unless specific penalties for particular combinations of missing items
are indicated.  These
takeoff, en route and landing penalties apply to the
most limiting corresponding weight.
If performance penalties are
not indicated for removed items, no more than three of such items
can be missing without taking further penalty. If more than three
of such items are missing together, the following performance penalties
are applicable per additional missing item:
Takeoff and
approach climb performance limiting weights are reduced by  50  ( 110 )
En route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  60  ( 132 ) (i.e. corresponding to a  60  decrease of en-route net ceiling).
Performance Penalties calculated with AFM_OCTO Software
The takeoff, approach climb and en route
performance with  MCDL  items missing can be determined by
selecting the missing items in the “CDL item...” menu of the AFM_OCTO
interface, using the database given in PERFORMANCE chapter ( ) of this  AFM  associated
to the CDLA320F.cdl file at issue 7 using AFM_OCTO approved FM module
at the revision 36 or higher.  Items
for which no performance penalty is indicated in this  MCDL  chapter are referenced as negligible items. Select the number of
negligible items in the “CDL item...” menu to determine performance
impact when four or more of such items are missing.
The most limiting
performance between the one computed with items missing and the one
computed without item missing must be used.
Ram Air Inlet Flap
All may be missing.
System performance
in heating mode will be decreased.
The following performance penalties
are applicable per missing flap:
Takeoff and
approach climb performance limiting weights are reduced by  190  ( 418 )
En route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  216  ( 476 )
Fuel consumption
is increased by  0.53 .

Static Discharger
20  of static dischargers
may be missing or inoperative on each the following areas:
RIGHT wing
LEFT wing
Vertical stabilizer
(including rudder)
RIGHT horizontal
stabilizer (including elevator)
LEFT horizontal
stabilizer (including elevator).
If a static
discharger is missing or inoperative on a flap track fairing, the  VHF  and  HF  sound quality may be slightly degraded.
When combined
with the case of dispatch with a wing tip fence missing,  20  of the remaining static dischargers of the affected wing are allowed
to be inoperative or missing ( ).

Refuel/Defuel Coupling Cap 101 QM Or 41 QM
All may be missing provided seal integrity of coupling is positively
confirmed (i.e. no fuel leak reported).
If the coupling
or O-ring is damaged and the cap cannot be fitted, dispatch is permitted
in accordance with this CDL item provided the cap is removed and coupling
integrity is verified.
If only the
lanyard is missing, or damaged, the removal of the cap and the application
of this MCDL item are not necessary.
May be combined
with MCDL item 28-02 ( ).

Wing Tip Fence
One lower part of a wing tip fence may be missing provided the broken
part is removed and holes are covered.
The static discharger that is
on the removed wing tip fence part must be considered inoperative
( ).
The following performance penalties
are applicable:
Takeoff and
approach climb performance limiting weights are reduced by  4480  ( 9877 )
En-route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  378  ( 834 )
Fuel consumption
is increased by  0.88 .
One complete wing tip fence may be missing provided that:
The associated
aileron is equipped with four static dischargers
The exposed
interior structure is covered
The protective
material is inspected before each flight and replaced if necessary.
The static dischargers that
are on the removed wing tip fence must be considered inoperative ( ).
The following performance penalties
are applicable:
Takeoff and
approach climb performance limiting weights are reduced by  10080  ( 22223 )
En-route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  756  ( 1667 )
Fuel consumption
is increased by  1.69 .

Lockout Pin
All may be missing from stowage position
provided the hole is covered.
Without lockout pin,
the associated thrust reverser cannot be deactivated.
Illustration Lockout Pin
For dispatch conditions:
Thrust Reverser Blocker Door
One may be missing provided the associated linkage is removed.
The associated thrust reverser
must be deactivated ( ).
The following performance penalties are applicable:
Takeoff and
approach climb performance limiting weights are reduced by  249  ( 549 )
Enroute performance
limiting weight is reduced by  166  ( 366 )
Fuel consumption
is increased by  0.36 .
Thrust Reverser Blocker Door
One blocker door per engine may be missing
provided that the corresponding drag link is removed ( ) and the affected Thrust Reverser
is deactivated ( ).
May be combined
with any other item listed in  MCDL -78 chapter.
May be combined with the following  MCDL  items provided that they are not located on the same engine:
78-44 ( ).

F/CTL - ALTN LAW (PROT LOST)
Maximum speed:  320 .
For approach and landing:
Use FLAPS 3 for landing.
Set  GPWS
LDG  FLAPS 3 to ON.
Approach speed =  VREF  +  10 .
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
When landing
gear extended and autopilots off, controls revert to direct law. Except
for the Yaw Damper loss where the controls remain in alternate law.
If manual pitch
trim not available, extend landing gear when in CONF 3 and at approach
speed in order to delay the reversion to direct law.
For go-around:
Set thrust levers
to  TOGA .

WING A.ICE - SYS FAULT or OFF
Turn off wing anti-ice.
Avoid icing conditions.
If severe ice accretion:
With anti-ice failed, the  AOA  protections are still efficient.
Minimum
speed:  VLS
+10 /G DOT.
For landing:
Apply necessary landing
performance correction.
Maneuver with care.
Avoid large roll inputs at high  AOA  and high thrust setting. In the case of abnormal response
in pitch or roll, release the backstick and reduce thrust.
WING A.ICE - SYS FAULT or OFF
In case of  WAI  low pressure:
Increase thrust.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off wing anti-ice.
Avoid icing conditions.
If severe ice accretion:
With anti-ice failed, the  AOA  protections are still efficient.
Minimum speed  VLS  +10/G DOT.
For landing:
Apply necessary landing
performance correction.
Maneuver with care.
Avoid large roll inputs at high  AOA  and high thrust setting. In the case of abnormal response
in pitch or roll, release backstick and reduce thrust.

WING A.ICE - SYS FAULT or OFF
Avoid icing conditions.
If severe ice accretion:
With anti-ice failed, the  AOA  protections are still efficient.
Minimum speed  VLS  +10/G DOT.
Maneuver with care.
Avoid large roll inputs at high  AOA  and high thrust setting. In the case of abnormal response
in pitch or roll, release backstick and reduce thrust.
Do not use speed brakes.
For landing:
Apply necessary landing
performance correction.
ENG RELIGHT IN FLIGHT
Maximum flight level:  300
Set affected engine master lever
to OFF.
Set affected engine thrust lever
to idle.
Set ENG MODE selector to IGN
START.
Open crossbleed valve if necessary.
Set engine master lever to ON.
Engine light up must be achieved
within  30  after fuel flow increases.
When idle reached:
Set ENG MODE selector
to NORM.
In Flight Engine Relight Envelope
ENG RELIGHT IN FLIGHT
Maximum flight level:  280
Set affected engine master lever
to OFF.
Set affected engine thrust lever
to idle.
Set ENG MODE selector to IGN
START.
Open crossbleed valve.
Set engine master lever to ON.
Engine light up must be achieved
within  30  after fuel flow increases.  During the relight
attempt, if the N2 did not increase, consider increasing speed for
windmill relight.
When idle reached:
Set ENG MODE selector
to NORM.
In Flight Engine Relight Envelope
For an
in-flight restart, the  FADEC  decides whether the rotor
speed is sufficient to perform a windmill relight or needs assistance
from the starter in view of the current engine parameters and the
flight environment parameters. Within the windmill quick relight envelope,
the  FADEC  will restart the engine after the flight crew
cycles the  ENG  MASTER lever from “ON” to “OFF” then back
to “ON”. The overall engine start time can be slightly longer than
2 minutes in the starter assisted relight envelope.
FORCED LANDING
Notify  ATC  and cabin
crew of the nature of emergency or use transponder (if available)
as required.
Prepare cockpit and cabin.
Turn off  TAWS  -  GPWS .
Turn on cabin signs.
Turn on emergency exit lights.
Turn off COMMERCIAL.
Manually set the landing elevation.
Turn on Emergency Locator Transmitter
( ELT ) (if installed), when conditions permit.
For approach:
Turn on RAM AIR.
Extend landing gear.
Use maximum available
slats/flaps.
Arm ground spoilers.
Maximum brake pressure:  1000 .
At  2000
AGL :
Notify cabin crew
for landing.
At  500
AGL :
Order brace for impact.
At touchdown:
Set both engine master
levers to OFF.
Shut down  APU .
After landing:
When aircraft stopped:
Set parking brake
to ON.
Notify  ATC  with  VHF  1.
Push all FIRE pushbuttons
(engines and  APU ).
Discharge all fire
agents (engines and  APU ).
If evacuation required:
Initiate evacuation.
If evacuation not required:
Notify cabin crew
and passengers to remain seated.

DITCHING
Notify  ATC  and cabin
crew of the nature of emergency or use transponder (if available)
as required.
Prepare cockpit and cabin.
Turn off  TAWS  -  GPWS .
Turn on cabin signs.
Turn on emergency exit lights.
Turn off COMMERCIAL.
Set landing elevation to sea
level.
Turn on Emergency Locator Transmitter
( ELT ) (if installed), when conditions permit.
For approach:
Keep landing gear
up.
Use maximum available
slats/flaps.
Set  ENG  MODE selector to NORM.
At  2000
AGL :
Check CAB PR MODE
SEL is in AUTO position.
Turn off all bleeds
(engines and  APU ).
Notify cabin crew
for ditching.
Set DITCHING pushbutton
to ON.
Touchdown should be made at  11  pitch attitude with minimum aircraft vertical speed.
At  500
AGL :
Order brace for impact.
At touchdown:
Set both engine master
levers to OFF.
Shut down  APU .
After ditching:
Notify  ATC  with  VHF  1.
Push all FIRE pushbuttons
(engines and  APU ).
Discharge all fire
agents (engines and  APU ).
Initiate evacuation.

BRAKES - A-SKID N-WS FAULT or OFF
Apply maximum brake pressure  1000 .
Nosewheel steering is inoperative.
Apply necessary landing performance
corrections.
FUEL GRAVITY FEEDING
Descend to gravity feed ceiling
(if applicable):
Flight conditions
at the time of gravity feeding
Gravity feed ceiling
Flight time above  300  greater than  30  (Fuel deaerated)
Current FL
Flight time above  300  lower than  30  (Fuel non-deaerated)
300
Aircraft flight level
never exceeded  300  (Fuel non-deaerated)
The highest of:
150
7000  above takeoff airport altitude
In case JET B fuel
is used, the gravity feed ceiling is  100  in all cases.
When reaching gravity feed ceiling:
Set ENG MODE selector
to IGN START.
Avoid negative g load
factor.
Close crossfeed valve.
If only one engine is running and is fed by gravity:
Open crossfeed valve.
Adjust bank angle  1  wing down on running engine side.
Use rudder trim to
get constant course and neutral stick.
When fuel imbalance at or above  1000  ( 2204 ):
Increase bank angle
to  2  or  3  wing down on running engine side.
Fuel from opposite
wing is used until imbalance is reduced to 0.

EMERGENCY EVACUATION
When aircraft stopped:
Set parking brake
to ON.
Notify  ATC  with  VHF  1.
Alert cabin crew.
If MAN CAB PR has been used:
Check cabin differential
pressure at zero before opening the doors.
Set both engine master
levers to OFF.
Push all FIRE pushbuttons
(engines and  APU ).
Discharge all fire
agents (engines and  APU ) as required.
If evacuation required:
Initiate evacuation.
If evacuation not required:
Notify cabin crew
and passengers to remain seated.
EMER DESCENT
Use crew oxygen masks.
Turn on cabin signs.
Initiate an emergency descent.
Set all thrust levers to idle
(if autothrust is not active).
Extend full speed brakes.
Descend at the maximum appropriate
speed.
If structural damage
suspected, reduce speed as appropriate and maneuver with care.
Set ENG START selector to  IGN  START.
Notify  ATC  of the
nature of emergency.
Notify the cabin of emergency
descent.
Consider squawk 7700 on  ATC  transponder.
Maximum flight level is the higher one of:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
If cabin altitude above  14000 :
Manually confirm passengers
oxygen masks on.
F/CTL - FLAPS LOCKED
Speed is limited to the  VFE  corresponding to the next
more extended flaps configuration.
.
If flaps position below 3:
Use FLAPS 3 for landing.
Turn off GPWS flap
mode.
If flaps position at 3:
Use FLAPS 3 for landing.
Turn on GPWS LDG FLAP
3.
If flaps position above 3:
Use FLAPS FULL for
landing.
Turn on GPWS LDG FLAP
3.
Apply necessary approach speed
and landing performance corrections.  .
F/CTL - FLAPS LOCKED
Speed is limited to the  VFE  corresponding to the next
more extended flaps configuration.
.
If flaps position below 3:
Set Flap lever to
position 3 for landing.
Turn off GPWS flap
mode.
If flaps position at 3:
Set Flap lever to
position 3 for landing.
Turn on GPWS LDG FLAP
3.
If flaps position above 3:
Set Flap lever to
position FULL for landing.
Turn on GPWS LDG FLAP
3.
Flaps will not move when Flap
lever is set for landing.
Apply necessary approach speed
and landing performance corrections.  .
F/CTL - SLATS LOCKED
Speed is limited to the  VFE  corresponding to the next
more extended slats configuration.
.
Use FLAPS 3 for landing.
Turn on GPWS LDG FLAP 3.
Apply necessary approach speed
and landing performance corrections.  .
If SLATS FAULT by double  SFCC  failure, flight controls are in alternate law.  .

F/CTL - SPLR FAULT
If spoilers 3+4 affected:
Do not use speed brakes.
For landing:
Apply necessary landing performance corrections.
If all spoilers
are failed, flight controls are in alternate law.  .
Due to the increased
drag, a descent may be needed to maintain the optimum speed : Green
Dot +  10 .
F/CTL - SEC FAULT
Turn off then on affected  SEC .
If reset not successful:
Turn off affected  SEC .
If  SEC  1 or  SEC  1+2 or  SEC  1+3 affected:
Do not
use speed brakes.
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
If  SEC  2 or  SEC  3 or  SEC  2+3
affected:
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
If all  SEC s are inoperative:
Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
Do not
use speed brakes.
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
HYD - G SYS LO PR
If hydraulic system not recovered:
For approach and landing:
Slats and flaps extend slowly.
Extend landing gear
by gravity.  .
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
Spoilers are partially inoperative.
HYD - Y SYS LO PR
If hydraulic system not recovered:
For approach and landing:
Flaps extend slowly.
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
Nosewheel steering
is inoperative.
Spoilers are
partially inoperative.
HYD - B SYS LO PR
If hydraulic system not recovered:
For approach and landing:
Slats extend slowly.
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
Spoilers are partially inoperative.
L/G GRAVITY EXTENSION
Pull and turn the gravity extension
handle and pull down landing gear lever.
Confirm landing gear locked
down.

CAB PR - SYS 1 + 2 FAULT
Use manual pressurization mode.
Monitor cabin altitude and differential
pressure are within limits.
During final approach, maintain
the cabin vertical speed switch in the UP position.
Check cabin differential pressure
at zero before opening doors.
UNRELIABLE AIRSPEED INDICATION
Unreliable airspeed indication
may be due to radome damage, or due to air probe failure or obstruction.
Below  250
If the safe conduct of the flight is impacted:
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
If below thrust reduction altitude:
Apply TOGA thrust.
Target a pitch attitude
of  15 .
If above thrust reduction altitude:
Apply CLB thrust.
When below  100 :
Target a pitch attitude
of  10 .
When above  100 :
Target a pitch attitude
of  5 .
If in CONF 0 or 1 or 2 or 3:
Maintain the slats/flaps
in their current configuration.
If in CONF FULL:
Select CONF3 and maintain.
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
When airborne:
Retract landing gear.
Respect stall
warning.
The  GPS  altitude appears on the MCDU GPS monitor page.
When flight path is stabilized:
Turn on probe and
window heat.
If faulty  ADR (s) cannot be identified or all  ADR s provide erroneous data:
Turn off all  ADR s.
Apply NAV - ADR 1+2+3
FAULT procedure.  .
Above  250
If the failure is due to radome
destruction, the drag will increased and therefore  N1  must
be increased by  5 .
If the safe conduct of the flight is impacted:
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
Apply CLB thrust.
Target a pitch attitude
of  5 .
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
Respect stall
warning.
The  GPS  altitude appears on the MCDU GPS monitor page.
When flight path is stabilized:
Turn on probe and
window heat.
If faulty  ADR (s) cannot be identified or all  ADR s provide erroneous data:
Keep one  ADR  on.
Adjust pitch attitude
and thrust regarding flight phase and aircraft configuration to obtain
and maintain target.
When below  250  and speed is still unreliable:
Turn off all  ADR s.
Apply NAV - ADR 1+2+3
FAULT procedure.  .
UNRELIABLE AIRSPEED INDICATION
Unreliable airspeed indication
may be caused by a radome destruction or obstructed pitots.
Below  250:
If the safe conduct of the flight is impacted:
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
If below thrust reduction altitude:
Apply TOGA thrust.
Set pitch attitude
to  15 .
If above thrust reduction altitude:
Apply CLB thrust.
When below  100 :
Set pitch attitude
to  10 .
When above  100 :
Set pitch attitude
to  5 .
If in CONF 0 or 1 or 2 or 3:
Maintain the slats/flaps
in their current configuration.
If in CONF FULL:
Select CONF3 and maintain.
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
When airborne:
Retract landing gear.
Use the  BKUP
SPD/ALT .
Fly the green area
of the speed scale.
Respect stall
warning.
If the  BUSS  does not react to longitudinal stick input when flying
the green area of the speed scale, the flight crew must disregard
the  BUSS  and adjust pitch attitude and thrust regarding
flight phase and aircraft configuration to obtain and maintain target.
When flight path is stabilized:
Turn on probe and
window heat.
If faulty  ADR (s) cannot be identified or all  ADR s provide erroneous data:
Keep one  ADR  on.
Above  250:
If the failure is due to radome
destruction, the drag will be increased and therefore  N1  must be increased by  5  in cruise.
If the safe conduct of the flight is impacted:
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
Apply CLB thrust.
Set pitch attitude
to  5 .
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
Respect stall
warning.
The  GPS  altitude appears on the MCDU GPS monitor page.
When flight path is stabilized:
Turn on probe and
window heat.
If faulty  ADR (s) cannot be identified or all  ADR s provide erroneous data:
Keep one  ADR  on.
Adjust pitch attitude
and thrust regarding flight phase and aircraft configuration to obtain
and maintain target.
When below  250  and speed is still unreliable:
Use the  BKUP
SPD/ALT .
Fly the green area
of the speed scale.
If the  BUSS  does
not react to longitudinal stick input when flying the green area of
the speed scale, the flight crew must disregard the  BUSS  and adjust pitch attitude and thrust regarding flight phase and
aircraft configuration to obtain and maintain target.
UNRELIABLE AIRSPEED INDICATION
Unreliable airspeed indication
may be due to radome damage, or due to air probe failure or obstruction.
If the failure is due to radome destruction, the drag will be increased
and therefore  N1  must be increased by  5 .
If the safe conduct of the flight is impacted:
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
If below thrust reduction altitude:
Apply TOGA thrust.
Target a pitch attitude
of  15 .
If above thrust reduction altitude:
Apply CLB thrust.
When below  100 :
Target a pitch attitude
of  10 .
When above  100 :
Target a pitch attitude
of  5 .
If in CONF 0 or 1 or 2 or 3:
Maintain the slats/flaps
in their current configuration.
If in CONF FULL:
Select CONF3 and maintain.
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
When airborne:
Retract landing gear.
Respect stall warning.
When flight path stabilized:
Turn on probe and
window heat.
Adjust pitch attitude and thrust
regarding flight phase and aircraft configuration to obtain and maintain
target speed.
UNRELIABLE AIRSPEED INDICATION
Unreliable airspeed indication
may be due to radome damage, or due to air probe failure or obstruction.
If the failure is due to radome destruction, the drag will be increased
and therefore  N1  must be increased by 5 %.
If the safe conduct of the flight is impacted:
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
If below thrust reduction altitude:
Apply TOGA thrust.
Target a pitch attitude
of  15 .
If above thrust reduction altitude:
Apply CLB thrust.
When below  100 :
Target a pitch attitude
of  10 .
When above  100 :
Target a pitch attitude
of  5 .
If in CONF 0 or 1 or 2 or 3:
Maintain the slats/flaps
in their current configuration.
If in CONF FULL:
Select CONF3 and maintain.
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
When airborne:
Retract landing gear.
If the NAV ALL SPD UNCERTAIN is  NOT  triggered:
Apply ECAM procedure(s).
Use reliable speed
data.
If the NAV ALL SPD UNCERTAIN is triggered:
At any time, if NAV AIR SPD STS CHANGED is displayed on  ECAM :
Apply  ECAM  procedure(s).
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
Adjust pitch attitude
and thrust regarding flight phase and aircraft configuration to obtain
and maintain target.
When flight path is stabilized:
Turn on probe and
window heat.
If faulty  ADR (s) cannot be identified or all  ADR s provide erroneous data:
Keep one  ADR  on.
For approach and landing:
Set Flap lever to
position 3 for landing  Approach speed = VLS
+  10
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections
ADR 1+2+3 FAULT
Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
Turn off all  ADR s.
Use standby instruments.
Automatic cabin pressurization system is inoperative.  .
Rudder travel
limiter is inoperative.  .
Extend landing gear by gravity.  .
FUEL LEAK
A fuel leak may be detected
if:
The sum of the  FOB  and the FU is significantly less than the  FOB  at engine start, or decreases, or
A passenger
observes fuel spray from engine/pylon or wing tip, or
The total fuel
quantity decreases at an abnormal rate, or
A fuel imbalance
develops, or
The fuel quantity
of a tank decreases too fast (leak from engine/pylon or a hole in
a tank), or
A tank overflows
(due to a pipe rupture in a tank), or
Fuel flow is
excessive (leak from engine), or
Fuel is smelt
in the cabin.
If visibility permits, a visual
check from the cabin may enable identification of the leak source.
When a leak is confirmed:
LAND ASAP
If leak from engine/pylon confirmed:
Shut down affected
engine.
If the leak stops, the wing
crossfeed valve can now be selected open to re-balance the fuel quantity
or to enable use of fuel from both wings. Do not restart the engine.
If leak from engine/pylon not confirmed or leak not located:
Keep wing crossfeed
valve closed.
Do not open the wing
crossfeed valve, even if requested by another  ECAM  procedure.
Close both center
tank transfer valves.
Monitor wing tank
fuel quantities and look for one tank depleting faster.
If one wing tank depletes faster than the other by at least  300  ( 661 ) in less than  30 :
Shut down affected
engine.
If the leak stops, the wing
crossfeed valve can now be selected open to re-balance the fuel quantity
or to enable use of fuel from both wings. Do not restart the engine.
Monitor fuel leak.
Open both center tank
transfer valves.
If the leak continues after
engine shutdown, suspect leak from wing, consider restarting the engine
and close center tank transfer valves on the leaking side. The crossfeed
valve can be selected open.
If both wing tanks deplete at a similar rate:
The wing crossfeed valve can
be selected open.
If fuel smell in the cabin:
If  APU  running:
Shut down  APU .
When wing tank fuel quantity below  3  ( 6614 ):
Open
both center tank transfer valves.
For landing:
Do not use reverse.

A fuel leak may be detected
if:

tank pumps.

FUEL ACT UNUSABLE
If only  ACT  1 unusable and  ACT  2 empty:
Confirm  ACT  mode to AUTO.
If only  ACT  2 unusable and  ACT  1 empty:
Confirm  ACT  mode to AUTO.
If  ZFCG  above  36.5  or not known:
Close center tank
transfer valves.
Disregard ECAM FUEL-CTR TK XFR
OFF procedure.  Disregard ECAM FUEL-AUTO
TRANSFER FAULT procedure.
If both  ACT  unusable:
Confirm  ACT  mode to AUTO.
If  ZFCG  between  30  and  32.5 :
Close center tank
transfer valves.
Disregard ECAM FUEL-CTR TK XFR
OFF procedure.  Disregard ECAM FUEL-AUTO
TRANSFER FAULT procedure.
If  ZFCG  above  32.5  or not known:
Close center tank
transfer valves.
Maximum
flight time:  2  30
Disregard ECAM FUEL-CTR TK XFR
OFF procedure.  Disregard ECAM FUEL-AUTO
TRANSFER FAULT procedure.

Operations in Icing Conditions
The engine anti-ice must be
ON during all ground and flight operations when icing conditions exist
or are anticipated, except during climb and cruise when the temperature
is below  -40
SAT .  The engine anti-ice must be ON prior to and during descent in icing
conditions, including temperatures below  -40
SAT .
Do not rely on airframe visual
icing cues to turn engine anti-ice on. Use the temperature and visual
moisture criteria specified the icing conditions definition ( ).
Delaying the use of
engine anti-ice until buildup is visible from the cockpit may result
in severe engine damage and/or flameout.

ELEC - EMER CONFIG (BOTH ENGINE GENERATORS FAILED)
Minimum  RAT  speed:  140
Turn off then on all generators
one after the other.
If no generator reset successful:
Set BUS TIE to OFF.
Attempt a further
all generators reset.
If any generator reset is successful:
Turn off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
If generator reset still unsuccessful:
For communications,
only  VHF  1 and  ATC  1 are available.
Flight controls
are in alternate law.  .
Engines are
fed by gravity.  .
The Cockpit
Door Locking System ( CDLS ) is inoperative (if installed).
Manually confirm emergency
electrical power on.
Set  ENG  MODE selector to IGN START.
Turn off then on  FAC  1.
Set BUS TIE to AUTO.
Start  APU  if available.
Set ventilation blower
and extract to OVRD.
For approach and landing:
Slats and flaps extend slowly.
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.
Antiskid is
inoperative.  .
Half spoilers
are inoperative.
ELEC - EMER CONFIG (BOTH ENGINE GENERATORS FAILED)
Minimum  RAT  speed:  140
Turn off then on all generators
one after the other.
If generator reset not successful:
Set BUS TIE to OFF.
Attempt a further
reset of all generators.
If any generator reset
is successful, turn off then on both  FAC s one after the
other.
Manually confirm emergency
electrical power on.

in alternate law.  .

only  VHF  1 and  ATC  1 are available.

fed by gravity.  .
Turn off then on  FAC  1.

and extract to OVRD.

Approach speed = VREF  +  15

performance corrections.

extend slowly.

inoperative.  .

are inoperative.

Door Locking System ( CDLS ) is inoperative (if installed).

ENG - FAIL
If no damage:
Attempt an immediate
engine relight setting ENG MODE selector to IGN START and engine thrust
lever to idle.
If no engine relight after  30 :
Shut down affected
engine.
Consider engine relight.  .
If engine damage:
Push relevant  FIRE  and discharge AGENT 1 after  10 .
At low speed, do not
rely only on N2 indication to assess engine damage.
ENG - FAIL
If no damage:
Attempt an immediate
engine relight setting ENG MODE selector to IGN START and engine thrust
lever to idle.
If no engine relight after  30 :
Shut down affected
engine.
Consider engine relight.  .
If engine damage:
Push relevant  FIRE  and discharge AGENT 1 after  10 .
At low speed, do not rely only
on N2 indication to assess engine damage.
ENG - FAIL
Retract landing gear (if not
already done).
If no damage:
Attempt an immediate
engine relight setting  ENG MODE  to IGN START and engine
thrust lever to idle.
If no engine relight after  30 :
Shut down affected
engine.
Consider engine relight.  .
If engine damage:
Push relevant  FIRE  and discharge AGENT 1 after  10 .

already done).

Takeoff Performance
For takeoff performance determination
on dry and wet runway, the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO
approved FM module at the latest approved revision must be used.  .
For takeoff performance
on wet runways the takeoff weight must be the lowest of the computed
one on dry runways and the computed one on wet runways.
Approach Climb and Landing Climb
The approach climb speed is at least
1.23  VS1G  of the approach configuration, approach speed
up to 1.41  VS1G  is permitted.  For approach and landing climb limiting
weight determination the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO
approved FM modules at the latest approved revision must be used.
The go around
gradient conditions in landing configuration with two engines operative
are never limiting.
When icing conditions are predicted
during the flight and  TAT  is below  10  and there is evidence of significant ice accretion,
to take into account ice formation on the non heated structure:
Decrease the
approach/landing climb limiting weight by  7.2 .
For landing, increase the approach
and landing speeds by:
10  in CONF 3
5  in CONF FULL
For landing
distance determination select ice accretion in the AFM_OCTO input
data Interface.

approach configuration, approach climb speed up to 1.31  VS1G  is permitted.  For approach and landing climb limiting weight determination, the
Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM modules at the
latest approved revision must be used,  .
The
go around gradient conditions in landing configuration with two engines
operative in TOGA thrust mode are never limiting.

on the non heated structure:
Decrease the
approach/landing climb limiting weight by  7.1 .
For landing
distance determination select ice accretion in the AFM_OCTO input
data Interface.
The all engines
go-around soft mode gradient in approach climb configuration is at
or above the approach climb gradient calculated by the Performance
Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module.
The go-around
soft mode gradient in landing climb configuration is the lower of  4  and the landing climb gradient calculated by the Performance Engineer's
Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module.

Takeoff Flight Path
AUTO FLT - RUD TRV LIM SYS
Use rudder with care above  140 .
Turn off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
Use differential braking if
necessary as soon as main gears are on ground.
Maximum crosswind for landing:  15 .
Do not use autobrake.
AUTO FLT - RUD TRV LIM SYS
Use rudder with care above  140 .
Turn off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
Use differential braking if
necessary as soon as main gears are on ground.
Maximum crosswind for landing:  15 .
Do not use autobrake.
AUTO FLT - RUD TRV LIM SYS
Use rudder with care above  160 .
Turn off then on  FAC  1.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off then on  FAC  2.
Use differential braking if
necessary as soon as main gears are on ground.
Maximum crosswind for landing:  15 .
Do not use autobrake.
AUTO FLT - RUD TRV LIM SYS
Use rudder with care above  140 .
Turn off then on  FAC  1.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off then on  FAC  2.
Use differential braking if
necessary as soon as main gears are on ground.
Maximum crosswind for landing:  15 .
Do not use autobrake.
General
This supplement is applicable
to dispatch the aircraft with inoperative items affecting the certified  AFM  performance.  The provision of performance
data in this supplement does not constitute authorization to operate
the aircraft with the specified items inoperative.  Unless amended in this supplement, all the chapters
of this  AFM  remain applicable.
FUEL - ACT XFR FAULT
Manually perform a forward fuel
transfer from  ACT .
Monitor transfer.
Descend to  270  if necessary.
When  ACT  empty:
lnterrupt manual transfer.
If no transfer:
Apply ACT UNUSABLE
procedure.  .
FUEL - ACT XFR FAULT
Manually perform a forward fuel
transfer from  ACT .
Monitor transfer.
Descend to  270  if necessary.
When  ACT  empty:
lnterrupt manual transfer.
If no transfer:
Apply ACT UNUSABLE
procedure.  .
FUEL - ACT XFR FAULT
Confirm ALL ACT pushbutton is
not set to ISOL.
Set ACT XFR rotary sel to appropriate  ACT .
Select the first  ACT  in the sequence FWD - AFT2- AFT1 which has not transferred.
Set ACT XFR MODE SEL to MAN.
When selected  ACT  contains less than  150  ( 330 ) of fuel for 5 minutes:
Set ACT XFR rotary
sel to next ACT in the sequence.
Repeat this step until all ACTs have
transferred.
All fuel remaining in the ACTs after
the manual transfer must be considered as unusable. Up to  150  ( 330 ) of fuel per ACT may be unusable.
When all ACTs have transferred:
Set ACT XFR to OFF.
Set ACT XFR MODE SEL
to AUTO.
If  ACT  does not manually transfer or if fuel  ACT  system fault:
Set ACT XFR to OFF.
Set ACT XFR MODE SEL
to MAN.
Close center tank
transfer valves.
Use only wing tank
fuel so as to keep the center of gravity within limits.
Disregard  FMS  fuel predictions as usable fuel is limited to wings only.
After failure detection, land before
the maximum flight time allowed by the following table:
Number of refuelled
ACTs at dispatch
1
2
3

-
-
120

-
120
170
AFT1 ACT XFR failed
120
170
200
FUEL FWD ACT ISOLATED
If no ENG FAlL warning:
Set FWD ACT to OVRD.
If ENG FAlL warning:
If FWD  ACT  not empty:
Set ACT XFR to OFF.
Set ACT XFR MODE SEL
to MAN.
Close center tank
transfer valves.
Use only wing tank
fuel so as to keep the center of gravity within limits.
Maximum Flight Time:  120
If FWD  ACT  empty:
Manually control  ACT  sequence.
Select each  ACT  one at a time.
Introduction
This aircraft must be operated
in compliance with the limitations given in this chapter. When operating
in accordance with an approved appendix or supplement to this  AFM , these limitations apply, unless amended by such appendix
or supplement.
Kind of Operations
The aircraft is certified in
the public transport category (passengers and freight) for day and
night operations, in the following conditions, when the appropriate
equipment and instruments required by the airworthiness
and operating regulations are approved, installed and in an operable
condition:
VFR  and  IFR
Extended overwater
flight
Flight in icing
conditions.
Minimum Flight Crew
Minimum flight crew: 2 pilots.
Maximum Operating Altitude
Slats and flaps retracted:  39800 .
This is the maximum
altitude at which it is possible to maintain cabin pressure altitude
below  8000 .
Slats and/or flaps extended:  20000 .
Maneuver Limit Load Factors
Slats and flaps retracted:  -1  to  +2.5 .
Slats and/or flaps extended:  0  to  +2.0 .
Performance Limitations
Maximum Takeoff Weight ( MTOW ) and Maximum Landing Weight ( MLW ) may be reduced
by performance requirements of PERFORMANCE and or SUPPLEMENTARY PERFORMANCE
chapters of this  AFM  related to :
Climb performance
(first and second segment, final takeoff, en route, approach and landing)
Available runway
length (takeoff and landing)
Obstacle clearance
(takeoff and en route)
Brake energy
limit (observe brake temperature warning ( 300 ))
Tire speed.
Loading
The aircraft must be loaded
in accordance with the loading instructions given in the Weight and
Balance Manual ( WBM ).  .
Airline Operation Control Applications
The Air Traffic Service Unit ( ATSU ) equipment has been approved for the provision of Airlines Operational
Control ( AOC ) applications.  The definition of the  AOC  application and obtaining its
subsequent approval, is the responsibility of the operator.
Mode S - EHS Enhanced Surveillance
The transponder mode S Enhanced Surveillance
( EHS ) has been demonstrated to comply with airworthiness
requirements contained in  ICAO  Doc 7030/4 for enhanced
surveillance in designated European airspace.
No credit should be
taken from extended squitter functionality until it has been granted
airworthiness and/or operational approval as applicable.
Mode S - EHS Enhanced Surveillance
The transponder system capable
of Mode A/Mode C, Elementary Surveillance ( ELS ) and Enhanced
Surveillance ( EHS ) has been demonstrated to comply with
the Annex II Part A and Part C of the Commission Regulation (EU) No
1207/2011, CS.ACNS.D.ELS (covering TGL 13) and CS.ACNS.D.EHS (covering
AMC 20-13).

squitter, Automatic Dependent Surveillance – Broadcast ( ADS-B ) Out function, has been demonstrated to comply with airworthiness

CS.ACNS.D.ADSB (covering  AMC  20-24), U.S 14  CFR  91.227 and  AC  20-165A. This approval is based on standards,

″ ADS-B  Out Capability Declaration″ document certification
reference 00D340P5333/C0S at the latest issue.

Areas.

General
This supplement is applicable to Extended
Operations ( ETOPS / EDTO ).
ETOPS / EDTO  requirements
apply to operations of two engines aircraft beyond the applicable
threshold specified by the national authority.  The type-design reliability and performance
of this aircraft-engine combination has been evaluated and found to
comply with the criteria of  AMC  20-6 ( ACJ  20X6/ AMJ  120-42/ IL  20) for operations between  60  and  120  diversion time when
the configuration, maintenance and procedures standards contained
in EASA approved Airbus ETOPS CMP document reference "SA/ EASA :  AMC  20-6/ CMP " at the latest
applicable revision are met.  This supplement
does not constitute an operational approval. Such authorization must
be obtained by the operator from the appropriate authorities.
Unless amended in this supplement, all
the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.

not exceed  120  at one engine cruising
speed, under standard conditions and still air.

systems is  195 .

Procedures
The procedures
given in the  EASA  approved Airbus  ETOPS
CMP  document are applicable.
NORMAL PROCEDURES
FUEL X FEED valve must be checked
before each  ETOPS  flight.
EMERGENCY AND ABNORMAL PROCEDURES
In addition to diversion cases covered in the EMERGENCY and
ABNORMAL PROCEDURES chapters of this  AFM  ( LAND ASAP ,  LAND ASAP  and fire procedures), diversion
becomes mandatory during  ETOPS  in case of:
only two generators
(either  IDG ,  APU
GEN  and  EMER
GEN ) remaining available, or
only two main
generators (either  IDG  or  APU
GEN ) remaining available and low level or low pressure or overheat on
blue hydraulic circuit.
In case of one  IDG  failure or one engine failure:
Start the  APU  and use the  APU  electrical channel.
In case of blue hydraulic circuit low level or low pressure
or overheat:
Start the  APU .
In case of electrical emergency configuration:
If one engine is failed, or if one bleed system is failed:
Forward cargo ventilation
(if installed) must be closed to avoid CAB PR-EXCESS CAB ALT being
triggered on  ECAM  during descent or cruise.
As all fuel pumps are lost, the engines
are fed by gravity.  .
Engine anti-ice valve are permanently
open.
In case of loss of air conditioning capability with the RAM
AIR open:
Switch off the CAB
FANS.

Note Definition
The following is the official definition
of a NOTE, taken directly from the  CS -25 (Chapter  AMC  25.1581, paragraph 3-e) and applicable to
Airbus flight operational documentation:
An operating procedure,
technique etc... considered essential to emphasize. Information contained
in notes may also be safety related.
General
The nominal runway width for
operations of the aircraft is  45 . Operations on runways
with a width below  45  are covered by modification
30397.
General
This supplement is applicable
to operations on runways with a width below  45 .  This supplement does not constitute an operational approval. Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.  Unless amended in this supplement,
all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
General
This supplement is applicable to operations on runways with a width
below  45 .  This aircraft has been shown
to be safely controllable and to be compliant with applicable airworthiness
requirements when operating on runways with a width of  30  or more.  This supplement does not constitute
an operational approval. Such authorization must be obtained by the
operator from the appropriate authorities.  Unless amended in this supplement, all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
General
This supplement is applicable
to operations on runways with a width below  45 .  This aircraft has been shown to be safely controllable and to be
compliant with applicable airworthiness requirements when operating
on runways with a width of  30  or more.  This supplement does not constitute an operational approval. Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.  Unless amended in this supplement,
all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
Performance
The performance for runways
with a width below  45  given in the approved
datafiles or databases of the PERFORMANCE chapter of this  AFM  must not be used for aircraft not fitted with modification 30397.
Performance
For performance determination, the Performance Engineer's Programs
AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved revision must be
used.
.  Enter the runway width in the
RUNWAY WIDTH field of the input data for  AFM  performance
calculation.  For runways with a width below  40 , the  VMCG  must be increased by:
Runway width
30
35
40
Δ VMCG  (kt)
+  2.5
+  1.5
+  0
and linear interpolation in between.
Performance
For performance determination,
the Performance Engineer's Programs AFM_OCTO approved FM module at
the latest approved revision must be used.
.  Enter the runway width in the RUNWAY WIDTH field of the input data
for  AFM  performance calculation.
Performance
For performance determination,
the Performance Engineer's Programs AFM_OCTO approved FM module at
the latest approved revision must be used.
.  Enter the runway width in the RUNWAY WIDTH field of the input data
for  AFM  performance calculation.
Limitations
Minimum runway width:  30
Systems:
The dispatch from/to narrow
runways is not allowed in case of:
nosewheel steering
inoperative
one brake or
more inoperative.
Autoland is
not allowed.
Introduction
In compliance with airworthiness regulations,
an aircraft is cleared to take off from any airport if the weight
allows it to achieve the takeoff, "en route", and landing
performance included in this chapter.
The performance and speeds of
the lowest weight at which the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO
approved FM module is able to give results can be considered as valid
from this weight down to the certified minimum weight.
The considered atmosphere is
the international standard atmosphere.  Performance are related to  VS1G .  Wind speed
is measured at the height of  10  ( 32.8 ).  The results provided by he Performance
Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module must be used in conjunction
with the gross weight, operational and environmental limitations given
in theLIMITATIONS chapter of this  AFM .

PW1133G-JM / PW1133GA-JM REDUCED TAKEOFF THRUST
Speeds Definitions
V1:
V1  is the highest
speed at which the decision must be made:
To continue
the takeoff, or
To stop the
aircraft.
VR:
VR  is the speed at which rotation
is initiated to reach  V2  before an altitude
of  35 .
V2:
V2  is the takeoff safety speed
reached before the altitude of  35  with one engine
failed and providing not less than the minimum second segment gradient
( 2.4 ).
Distances Definitions
STOPWAY (SWY):
Extension to runway, adequate for deceleration
of the aircraft in the case of aborted takeoff.
CLEARWAY (CWY):
Area beyond the runway which can be taken
into account for  TOD  calculation.
TAKEOFF DISTANCE AVAILABLE (TODA):
Sum of the  TORA  and the  CWY  available.
TAKEOFF RUN AVAILABLE (TORA):
Length of runway available and suitable
for the ground run of an aircraft taking off.
ACCELERATE-STOP DISTANCE AVAILABLE (ASDA):
Sum of the  TORA  and the  SWY  available.
TAKEOFF DISTANCE (TOD):
Distance covered from the brake release
to a point at which the aircraft is at the  35  height ( 15  height on wet runway). The  TOD  must not exceed the  TODA .
TAKEOFF RUN (TOR):
Distance covered from the brake release
to a point at which the aircraft is half of the segment between the
liftoff speed ( VLOF ) and  35  height ( 15  height on wet runway). The  TOR  must
not exceed the  TORA .
ACCELERATE-STOP DISTANCE (ASD):
Distance necessary to accelerate the
aircraft to  V1 , reject the takeoff at  V1  and
come to a full stop.  The  ASD  must not exceed the  ASDA .
In-Flight Performance
For en route net flight path
(single engine cruise) performance determination, the Performance
Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved
revision must be used.
In the case of severe icing
conditions, ice accretion may occur on aircraft surfaces that are
not heated. If ice accretion occurs, reduce the one-engine inoperative
net ceiling by  2000 .
In-Flight Performance
For en route net flight path (single
engine cruise) performance determination, the Performance Engineer's
Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved revision
must be used.  .
In the case of severe icing
conditions, ice accretion may occur on aircraft surfaces that are
not heated. If ice accretion occurs, reduce the one-engine inoperative
net ceiling by the following amount, as applicable:
By  4000  when the aircraft weight is below or equal to  60000  ( 132277 )
By  7500  when the aircraft weight is above or equal to  75000  ( 165347 ).
Apply a linear
interpolation between these two aircraft weights.
In-Flight Performance
For en route net flight path (single
engine cruise) performance determination, the Performance Engineer's
Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved revision
must be used.  .
In the case of severe icing
conditions, ice accretion may occur on aircraft surfaces that are
not heated. If ice accretion occurs, reduce the one-engine inoperative
net ceiling by  2400 .
In-Flight Performance
For en route net flight path
(single engine cruise) performance determination, the Performance
Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved
revision must be used, .
In the case of severe
icing conditions, ice accretion may occur on aircraft surfaces that
are not heated. If ice accretion occurs, reduce the one-engine inoperative
net ceiling by:
5000  for aircraft weight below or equal to  45000  ( 99207 ),
8000  for aircraft weight above  75000  ( 165346 ),
Linear interpolation
must be applied between the two aircraft weights.

net ceiling by:
6000  for aircraft
weight below or equal to  55000  ( 121254 ),
10500  for aircraft
weight above  80000  ( 176370 ),
Linear interpolation
must be applied between the two aircraft weights.

In-Flight Performance
For en route net flight path
(single engine cruise) performance determination, the Performance
Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved
revision must be used.
In the case of severe
icing conditions, ice accretion may occur on aircraft surfaces that
are not heated. If ice accretion occurs, reduce the one-engine inoperative
net ceiling by:
6500  for aircraft weight below or equal to  50000  ( 110231 ),
9500  for aircraft weight above  80000  ( 176370 ),
Apply linear
interpolation between the two aircraft weights.
Approach and Landing Speeds Definition
The final approach speed (landing
speed) is the minimum recommended speed at  50  height for normal
landing. It is equal to 1.23  VS1G  of the landing
configuration.
Approach and Landing Speeds
The final approach speed (landing
speed) is the minimum recommended speed at  50  height for normal
landing. It is equal to 1.23  VS1G  of the landing configuration.
To take into account ice formation
on the non-heated structure:
The minimum speed is:
VLS  +  5  in CONF FULL
VLS  +  10  in CONF below FULL
For landing
distance determination, select "Ice accretion" in the AFM_OCTO
input data interface.
Landing Distance Definitions
ACTUAL LANDING DISTANCE (ALD)
The Actual Landing Distance ( ALD ) represents the distance from the  50  height point to
complete stop on a smooth, dry, hard-surfaced runway. It is determined
with brake pedals depressed at main landing gear touch down, and assumes
the use of ground spoilers and antiskid. In normal operation, the
use of thrust reversers is recommended.
REQUIRED LANDING DISTANCE (rld)
The Required Landing Distance ( RLD ) is the Actual Landing Distance ( ALD ) divided by 0.6
assuming the surface is dry.  Under
wet runway conditions, the Required Landing Distance
( RLD ) is increased by  15 .

Introduction
The Airplane Flight Manual (AFM) is an
approved document that contains information (limitations, operating
procedures, performance information, etc.) necessary to operate the
airplane at the level of safety established by the airplane’s certification
basis. It is not established as an operational document to be used
directly by the crew in flight.  Flight crew documents available
in flight must include an Operational Manual, with appropriate contents
and language as required by the National Regulations.
Any Flight Crew Operating
Manual ( FCOM ) reference within the  AFM  must be considered as advisory information, the  FCOM  being
a non approved document.
This  AFM  is specific to a given certified aircraft model, which
is specified in the Heading Approbation Documentary Unit ( ).  It was approved by the Direction Générale
de l'Aviation Civile ( DGAC ) prior to 28 September
2003 and is since approved by the European Aviation Safety Agency
( EASA ), or by Airbus under Design Organisation Approval
( DOA ) reference EASA.21J.031 for  AFM  minor changes.
When applicable, it is approved by the  EASA  on behalf and
according to the requirements of the importing Authority, e.g. the
US Federal Aviation Administration ( FAA ) for US registered
aircraft.
Introduction
The Airplane Flight Manual (AFM) is an
approved document that contains information (limitations, operating
procedures, performance information, etc.) necessary to operate the
airplane at the level of safety established by the airplane’s certification
basis. It is not established as an operational document to be used
directly by the crew in flight.  Flight crew documents available
in flight must include an Operational Manual, with appropriate contents
and language as required by the National Regulations.
Any Flight Crew Operating Manual
( FCOM ) reference within the  AFM  must be considered
as advisory information, the  FCOM  being a not approved
document.
This  AFM  is specific to a given certified aircraft model, which
is specified in the Heading Approbation Documentary Unit ( ).  It is approved by the European Aviation
Safety Agency ( EASA ) or by Airbus under Design Organisation
Approval ( DOA ) reference EASA.21J.031 for  AFM  minor changes. When applicable, it is approved by the  EASA  on behalf and according to the requirements of the importing Authority,
e.g. the US Federal Aviation Administration ( FAA ) for US-registered
aircraft.
Customized AFM
The customized airline  AFM :
Is extracted
from a non customized aircraft model envelope  AFM
Is an approved
document related to an operator's/owner's fleet
Takes into account
the specific configuration of each aircraft of the concerned fleet.
The technical
content of this document is approved under the authority of the  DOA  ref.  EASA .21J.031.  Airbus
will provide a manual which reflects the aircraft configuration at
delivery, and the necessary revisions to reflect configuration changes
due to Airbus approved modifications.  The operator/owner
must inform Airbus without delay of the effective changes to the aircraft
delivery configuration made through Airbus Service Bulletin ( SB ). This allows Airbus to provide  AFM  revisions/updates
to the operator/owner.
Airbus will not provide revisions,
and thus will not assume the responsibility of any effect on the  AFM :
Due to modifications
installed by third parties without an Airbus  SB , and/or
Due to modifications
installed through an Airbus  SB , if Airbus is not informed
of the  SB  embodiment.
Procedures
Diversion to an at least  45  wide runway is recommended in case of:
Rudder jam
Rudder pedal
jam
Yaw damper fault
Rudder Travel
Limit system fault
All failures
leading to the loss of the nosewheel steering (HYD Yellow system loss,
double hydraulic failure, double  BSCU  fault, double  LGCIU  fault).
The following applies to narrow
runways in terms of crosswind component:
Maximum demonstrated
crosswind for dry runways:  38  (gust included) for takeoff and
landing
Maximum demonstrated
crosswind for wet runways:  33  (gust included) for takeoff and
landing
Maximum crosswind
for contaminated runways:  10  (gust included) for takeoff and
landing.
Operations on icy runways have not been
demonstrated.
Procedures
Diversion to an at least  45  wide runway is recommended in case of:
Rudder jam
Rudder pedal
jam
Yaw damper fault
Rudder Travel
Limit system fault
All failures
leading to the loss of the nosewheel steering (HYD Yellow system loss,
double hydraulic failure, double  BSCU  fault, double  LGCIU  fault).
The following applies to narrow
runways in terms of maximum crosswind component:
For dry runways:  38  (gust included) for takeoff and landing
For wet runways:  33  (gust included) for takeoff and landing
For contaminated
runways:  10  (gust included) for takeoff and landing.
These values are based on the
assumption that the flight crews are trained accordingly.
Operations on icy runways have not been demonstrated.
Organization of the Manual
The  AFM  is divided
into 11 chapters:
Approval data
General
Limitations
Emergency procedures
Abnormal procedures
Normal procedures
Performance
(dry and wet runway)
Appendices and
supplements
Master Configuration
Deviation List ( MCDL )
Supplementary
performance (contaminated runway)
Regulatory differences
(when applicable).
Documentary Unit (DU)
The  AFM  is made of Documentary
Units ( DU ). The  DU  is the smallest
part of information with a technical content.  The  DU s are listed on a separate "List of Effective Documentary
Unit" ( LEDU ).  The  LEDU  is produced
and approved by Airbus under the authority of  DOA  reference
EASA.21J.031.
Identification Strip
Below the title of the  DU ,
an identification strip is provided with each  DU  and consists
of:
Ident.: Each  DU  is identified by its own unique identification number
The approval
date of the  DU
The approval
marking
For non approved  DU , this field is replaced by the label: FOR INFORMATION ONLY.
Criteria: This
field provides the type of aircraft and associated configuration for
which the  DU  is applicable
SA  stands for Single
Aisle meaning that this  DU  is applicable to all A318/A319/A320/A321
family.
Specific: When
necessary, this field provides the code of the specific regulation
applicable to the  DU
Impacted by  TDU : When applicable, this field provides the identification
number and the title of the Temporary Documentary Unit ( TDU ) impacting the  DU
Belonging to:
When applicable, this field provides the number of  TR  to
which the  TDU  belongs
Impacted  DU : When applicable, this field provides the identification
number and the title of the  DU  impacted by the  TDU .
Temporary Revision (TR)
A Temporary Revision ( TR )
is related to amendments to be quickly approved and introduced in
the  AFM . A  TR  is made of Temporary Documentary
Units ( TDU ).
The  TR s are indicated with a dedicated layout and are
listed on a separate "List of Effective Temporary Revisions" ( LETR ).  A  TR  always has priority over the  AFM  content modified
by the  TR  (a  TDU  always has priority over the
content of the impacted  DU ).
Warning Definition
The following is the official definition
of a WARNING, taken directly from the  CS -25 (Chapter  AMC  25.1581, paragraph 3-e) and applicable to
Airbus flight operational documentation:
An operating procedure,
technique, etc... which may result in personal injury or loss of life
if not carefully followed.
Caution Definition
The following is the official definition
of a CAUTION, taken directly from the  CS -25
(Chapter  AMC  25.1581, paragraph 3-e) and applicable to
Airbus flight operational documentation:
An operating procedure, technique, etc... which may result in damage to
equipment if not carefully followed.
BOMB ON BOARD
Flight crew procedures
Establish communication between
cockpit and cabin.
If landing and evacuation possible within  30 :
Notify  ATC  / Company.
Land and initiate
evacuation.
If not possible:
Level off.
Manually control the
cabin pressure in order not to increase the cabin altitude.
Notify  ATC  / Company.
Descend the aircraft
in order to reach aircraft altitude equal to the higher one of: cabin
altitude +  2500  or  MEA .
Avoid sharp maneuvers.
Maximum cabin differential pressure:  1 .
When aircraft altitude equal to cabin altitude +  2500  or  MEA :
Maintain cabin differential
pressure at  1 .
Turn off galleys.
When bomb is secured at the  LRBL  or if bomb cannot
be moved:
Turn on emergency
exit light.
Turn off COMMERCIAL.
If fuel permits:
Use at least FLAPS
1.
Extend landing gear.
For approach and landing:
Set cabin altitude
mode to AUTO.
Reduce the differential
pressure to zero.
If evacuation required:
Initiate evacuation.
If evacuation not required:
Notify cabin crew
and passengers.
Cabin crew procedures
If landing and evacuation not possible within  30 :
If the bomb can be
moved, move it to the prepared Least Risk Bomb Location (centre of
the right hand aft cabin door).
General
The nominal runway surface for
performance calculation on wet runway is smooth.  The takeoff performance improvements
on wet grooved or Porous Friction Course ( PFC ) runways
are covered by modification 35075.  The landing performance improvements
on wet grooved/ PFC  runways are covered by modification
37457.
General
This supplement is applicable
to wet runway takeoff and landing when the runway surface is grooved
or  PFC  (Porous Friction Course).  The takeoff and landing performance on
wet grooved/ PFC  runways are approved provided the runways
are maintained to acceptable standards for grooved/ PFC  runways
in accordance with  FAA
AC 150/5320-12 or  CAA
CAP  683 or equivalent.  This supplement does not constitute an operational approval. Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.  Unless
amended in this supplement, all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
General
The nominal runway surface for
performance calculation on wet runway is smooth.  The takeoff performance improvements on wet grooved or Porous Friction
Course ( PFC ) runways are covered by modification 35075.  The landing performance improvements on wet grooved/ PFC  runways are covered by modification 37457.
Performance
The takeoff performance (Accelerate Stop
Distance, Complete Takeoff) on wet grooved/ PFC  runway given
in the approved databases of the PERFORMANCE chapter must not be used
for aircraft not fitted with modification 35075.  The landing performance (Landing Distance)
on wet grooved/ PFC  runway given in the approved
databases of the PERFORMANCE chapter must not be used for aircraft
not fitted with modification 37457.
Performance
For takeoff and landing performance determination,
the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved
FM module at the latest approved revision must be used.  .
Select
wet in the RUNWAY CONDITION field, then grooved/ PFC  in
the RUNWAY SURFACE field of the input data for Accelerated Stop Distance
( ASD ), Complete Takeoff and Landing Distance calculation.
For takeoff performance computation
on wet grooved/ PFC , stop way must not be considered except
if the stop way is grooved/ PFC .
Performance
The takeoff performance (Accelerate
Stop Distance, Complete Takeoff) on wet grooved/ PFC  runway
given in the approved databases of the PERFORMANCE chapter must not
be used for aircraft not fitted with modification 35075.  The landing performance (Landing Distance) on wet grooved/ PFC  runway given in the approved databases of the PERFORMANCE chapter
must not be used for aircraft not fitted with modification 37457.
General
NOISE CHARACTERISTICS
No determination has been made
by the  EASA  that the noise levels of this aircraft are
or should be acceptable for operation at, into, or out of, any airport.
NOISE LEVELS
Noise levels shown in this supplement
comply with  EASA
CS -36,  JAR  36, 14  CFR  ( FAR ) Part 36 Stage 4, and  ICAO  Annex
16 Chapter 4, noise requirements and were obtained by analysis of
approved data from approved noise tests.
Identification of the maximum takeoff
and landing weights applicable to a particular aircraft is provided
in the LIMITATIONS chapter of this  AFM  ( ).
NOISE CERTIFICATION PROCEDURES
Compliance with  EASA
CS -36,  JAR  36, 14  CFR  ( FAR ) Part 36, and  ICAO  Annex 16 included the following procedures:
An all-engine
takeoff configuration 1 + F was used with a constant climb speed equal
to the all-engine operating speed at  35 , which is at least  V2  +  10  and not greater than  V2  +  20 , with a thrust cutback procedure initiated prior to over-flight
of the flyover noise control point, with  APU  off, air conditioning
system off, mid center of gravity, and landing gear retracted.
Landing approach
on a  3  glide slope, at a speed of  VREF  +  10 , was used with  APU  on, air conditioning system on, forward
center of gravity, landing gear extended and configuration FULL.

center of gravity, landing gear extended.

on a  3  glide slope, at a speed of  VREF  +  10 , was used with  APU  on, air conditioning system on, forward
center of gravity, landing gear extended and configuration FULL.
General
NOISE CHARACTERISTICS
No determination has been made
by the  EASA  that the noise levels of this aircraft are
or should be acceptable or unacceptable for operation at, into, or
out of, any airport.
NOISE LEVELS
Noise levels shown in this supplement
comply with  EASA
CS -36, 14  CFR  Part
36 Stage 5, and  ICAO  Annex 16 Volume 1 Chapter 14, noise
requirements and were obtained by analysis of approved data from approved
noise tests.
Identification of the maximum
takeoff and landing weights applicable to a particular aircraft is
provided in the LIMITATIONS chapter of this  AFM  ( ).
NOISE CERTIFICATION PROCEDURES
Compliance with  EASA
CS -36, 14  CFR  Part 36 or  ICAO  Annex
16 Volume 1 Chapter 14 included the following procedures:
An all-engine
takeoff configuration 1 + F was used with a constant climb speed equal
to the all-engine operating speed at  35 , which is at least  V2  +  10  and not greater than  V2  +  20 , with a thrust cutback procedure initiated before overflying the
flyover noise control point and with  APU  off, air conditioning
system on, middle center of gravity and gear retracted.
Landing approach
on a  3  glide slope, at a speed of  VREF  +  10 , was used with  APU  on, air conditioning system on, forward
center of gravity, landing gear extended and configuration FULL.

INTRODUCTION
The procedures contained in this chapter
have been established and are recommended by the aircraft manufacturer
for application in the event of a serious failure.
The following important
remarks apply:
It is assumed
that, in general, all failures are indicated by the operation of specific
system warnings and/or by direct observation.
The actions
recommended may result in the loss of certain systems not associated
with the failure.
For a definition
of LAND ASAP,  .
When actions depend on a condition, a
dot (•) or a square (■) identifies this condition. The square is used
when there is a choice between one or more conditions and only one
is applicable.  These
procedures are approved by the Airworthiness Authorities as acceptable
procedures, for operation of the aircraft. This approval does not
prevent the operator from developing equivalent procedures provided
these procedures are approved by appropriate Operational Authorities.  In case of discrepancy between procedures
displayed on the  ECAM  and procedures stated in the  AFM , the  AFM  procedures always have precedence.  Unless otherwise specified in the procedures,
the minimum speed to be used for approach and landing is the  VLS  corresponding to the configuration requested by the procedure.
VLS , when mentioned
in a procedure, is the one corresponding to the configuration requested
by the procedure (e.g. if the procedure requests to use FLAPS 2, take  VLS  of CONF 2).
GENERAL
Whenever fire is encountered on the aircraft,
landing at the nearest airport is recommended.  After conducting any fire suppression/smoke
evacuation procedure, even though smoke has dissipated, if it has
not or cannot be visibly verified that the fire has been put out,
immediately land at the nearest airport.  The flight crew should always go to  100  oxygen whenever a hand held fire extinguisher is to be discharged
in the cockpit or when required because of smoke accumulation.
ENG - 1(2) OIL LO PR
If OIL PR below  60 :
Shut down affected
engine.
ENG - 1(2) OIL LO PR
If OIL PR below the second threshold:
Shut down affected
engine.
ENG - 1(2) OIL LO PR
If OIL PR below the red threshold:
Shut down affected
engine.
ENG 1 (2) FIRE (In Flight)
Shut down affected engine and
push relevant FIRE pushbutton.
Discharge AGENT 1 after  10 .
Notify  ATC .
If fire not extinguished after  30 :
Discharge AGENT 2.
ENG 1 (2) FIRE (On Ground)
Set all thrust levers to idle.
Full thrust reversers may be
used to stop the aircraft.
When aircraft stopped:
Set parking brake
to ON.
Notify  ATC .
Alert
cabin crew.
Shut down affected
engine and push relevant FIRE pushbutton.
Discharge all fire
agents of affected engine.
Shut down other engine.
If evacuation required:
Initiate evacuation.
Shut down  APU .
APU FIRE
Push  APU  FIRE pushbutton.
Discharge agent after  10 .
Shut down  APU .
SMOKE/FUMES/AVNCS SMOKE
If perceptible smoke is confirmed:
Use crew oxygen masks,
if required.
Set ventilation blower
and extract to OVRD.
Turn off cabin fans
and galleys.
Turn on cabin signs.
Establish communication
between cockpit and cabin.
Try to identify and
isolate faulty equipment.
If smoke source not immediately isolated:
Initiate a diversion.
Initiate a descent
to the higher one of:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
At any time of the procedure, if smoke/fumes becomes the greatest
threat:
Consider applying
the removal of smoke/fumes procedure and setting Elec Emer Config.  .
To set Elec Emer Config, turn
off EMER ELEC GEN 1 LINE and set EMER ELEC PWR to MAN ON then, when
EMER GEN is available, turn off  GEN  2 and  APU
GEN .  Apply the Elec Emer
Config procedure without performing generator reset.  .  Before landing gear
extension, restore normal electrical supply for landing: turn on GEN
2 and EMER ELEC GEN 1 LINE.
At any time of the procedure, if the situation becomes unmanageable:
Consider landing immediately.
If air conditioning smoke suspected:
Turn off  APU  bleed.
Set ventilation blower
and extract to AUTO.
Close all cargo isolation
valves.
Turn off pack 1.
If smoke continues:
Turn on pack 1.
Turn off pack 2.
If smoke still continues:
Turn on pack 2.
Set ventilation blower
and extract to OVRD.
Consider removal of
smoke/fumes procedure.  .
If cabin equipment smoke suspected:
If smoke continues:
Turn on emergency
exit lights.
Turn off COMMERCIAL.
Check smoke dissipation
and try to identify and isolate faulty equipment.
If smoke still continues or when faulty equipment confirmed
isolated:
Turn on COMMERCIAL.
Consider removal of
smoke/fumes procedure.  .
If smoke source cannot be determined and still continues or
avionics/electrical smoke suspected:
Consider setting Elec
Emer Config.
To set Elec Emer Config, turn
off EMER ELEC GEN 1 LINE and set EMER ELEC PWR to MAN ON then, when
EMER GEN is available, turn off  GEN  2 and  APU
GEN .  Apply the Elec Emer
Config procedure without performing generator reset.  .
3  before
landing or  2000  above airport altitude, restore normal electrical
supply for landing: turn on  GEN  2 and EMER ELEC GEN 1 LINE.
If smoke disappears within  5 :
Restore normal ventilation.
REMOVAL OF SMOKE/FUMES
Turn on emergency exit lights.
If fuel vapors:
Turn on CAB FANS.
Turn off both packs.
If no fuel vapors:
Turn off CAB FANS.
Set PACK FLOW to HI.
Set landing elevation to the
higher one of:  10000  or  MEA - MORA .
Descend to the higher one of:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
Notify  ATC .
While descending, continue applying
the appropriate steps of the smoke/fumes/avncs smoke procedure.
When at  100  or  MEA - MORA :
If electrical emergency configuration:
Set  APU  master switch to ON.
Turn off both packs.
Use manual pressurization
mode.
Maintain the cabin
vertical speed switch in the UP position.
Turn on RAM AIR when
cabin differential pressure below  1 .
Set  APU  master switch to OFF.
If smoke persists and  PM  cockpit window opening
required:
Maximum
speed:  200
Open cockpit door.
Put headsets on.
Open  PM  cockpit window.
When window open:
Turn on non affected
pack(s) and continue applying the appropriate steps of the smoke/fumes/avncs
smoke procedure.
Due to the increased noise level pay
particular attention to visual warnings.

Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
Disconnect autopilot.
Turn off flight directors.
Disconnect autothrust.
Turn on probe and window heat.
Turn off all  ADR s.
Fly the green area of the speed
scale.
Standby instruments
may be unreliable.
The altitude
displayed on the  PFD  is a  GPS  altitude.
Automatic cabin
pressurization system is inoperative.  .
Rudder travel
limiter is inoperative.  .
If the  BUSS  does not react to longitudinal stick input when flying
the green area of the speed scale, the flight crew must disregard
the  BUSS  and adjust pitch attitude and thrust regarding
flight phase and aircraft configuration to obtain and maintain target.
Do not use speed brakes.
Maneuver with care.
When FLAPS 2:
Extend landing gear
by gravity.  .
Approach speed: fly the bug.
Apply necessary landing performance
corrections.

L/G - GEAR NOT DOWNLOCKED
Recycle landing gear.
If unsuccessful after  2 :
Extend landing gear
by gravity.  .
CAB PR - EXCESS CAB ALT
If above  100 :
Use crew oxygen masks.
If below  160 :
Initiate descent to
the higher one of:  100  or  MEA-MORA .
Notify the cabin crew.
If above  160 :
Turn on cabin signs.
Initiate emergency
descent to the higher one of:  100  or  MEA-MORA .
If cabin altitude above  14000 :
Manually confirm passengers
oxygen masks on.
Electrical
Batteries conditions must be
checked before the first flight of the day.
BAT 1+2 charge current must
be reducing to a value below  60  after
BAT 1+2 OFF then ON.
Cockpit Door
If required by local Airworthiness Authorities:
Check that the cockpit
door is closed and locked before each flight.
Braking Efficiency
Test
When starting to taxi or after any  BSCU  reset on
ground:
As soon as the aircraft
starts to move, check the efficiency of the braking system by pressing
brake pedals.
Takeoff Procedure
Set slats, flaps and horizontal
stabilizer as required.
Perform flight controls checks
using the pedals and each sidestick.
Arm ground spoilers and select
maximum autobrake.
Set ENG MODE selector as required.
Apply 1.05  EPR  on
both engines with brakes on.
Brakes may be released so as
to perform a rolling takeoff.
If crosswind at or below  20  and no tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick half forward.
Apply thrust up to
FLX/TOGA thrust whilst maintaining stick half forward up to  80 .
If crosswind above  20  or tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick full forward.
Apply thrust progressively
to get FLX/TOGA thrust at  40  ground speed, whilst maintaining
stick close to full forward up to  80 .
Then release stick progressively
to reach neutral at  100 .
Once throttle
is set to FLX MCT gate, any change to FLEX TEMP setting will not be
taken into account by  FADEC  for  EPR  TARGET computation.
The  FADEC  includes a Keep-Out Zone ( KOZ ) which prevents the engine
from being stabilized between an approximate range of  60  to  74
N1  (limits are a function of the
outside air temperature). Therefore, during engine acceleration on
ground, the pilot may notice a non-linear thrust response to thrust
lever movement.
Check takeoff  EPR  is set prior to reach  80 .
At  VR :
Rotate the aircraft
with a positive sidestick input to achieve a normal and continuous
rotation rate to the pitch attitude necessary to maintain an airspeed
at or above  V2  +  10 .
Once airborne and with a positive rate of climb:
Retract landing gear.
SRS  guidance should be followed
when  FD  pitch order is established.
At safe height:
Accelerate up to at
least 1.18  VS1G  of configuration (1+F) or ″F″ speed if
CONF 2 or 3 in use.
Select slats/flaps
position 1 and accelerate up to at least 1.23  VS1G  of clean
configuration ("S" speed).
Select slats/flaps
position 0 and accelerate up to 1.36  VS1G  ("green
dot" speed).
Disarm ground spoilers.
For takeoff
in configuration 1 (1+F), F speed is not displayed.
If takeoff is
performed with packs off, pack 1 should be selected ON after thrust
reduction to CLB.
Takeoff Procedure
Set slats, flaps and horizontal
stabilizer as required.
Perform flight controls checks
using the pedals and each sidestick.
Arm ground spoilers and select
maximum autobrake.
Set ENG MODE selector as required.
Apply  50
N1  on both engines with brakes on.
Brakes may be released
so as to perform a rolling takeoff.
If crosswind at or below  20  and no tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick half forward.
Apply thrust up to
the FLX/TOGA thrust whilst maintaining stick half forward up to  80 .
If crosswind above  20  or tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick full forward.
Apply thrust rapidly
to  70
N1 , then progressively to get FLX/TOGA thrust
at  40  ground speed, whilst maintaining stick close to full
forward up to  80 .
Then release stick progressively
to reach neutral at  100 .
Once throttle is set to FLX
MCT gate, any change to FLEX TEMP setting will not be taken into account
by  FADEC  for  N1  TARGET computation.
Check takeoff  N1  is
set prior to reach  80 .
At  VR :
Rotate the aircraft
with a positive sidestick input to achieve a normal and continuous
rotation rate to the pitch attitude necessary to maintain an airspeed
at or above  V2  +  10 .
Once airborne and with a positive rate of climb:
Retract landing gear.
SRS  guidance should be followed when  FD  pitch
order is established.
At safe height:
Accelerate up to at
least 1.18  VS1G  of configuration (1+F) or ″F″ speed if
CONF 2 or 3 in use.
Select slats/flaps
position 1 and accelerate up to at least 1.23  VS1G  of clean
configuration ("S" speed).

position 0 and accelerate up to 1.36  VS1G  ("green
dot" speed).

in configuration 1 (1+F), F speed is not displayed.

is performed with packs off, pack 1 should be selected ON after thrust
reduction to CLB.

General
This supplement is applicable to operations
with the  TCAS .
TCAS  Traffic Advisory and Resolution Advisory modes can be used without
any restriction when operating within the certified limits of the
aircraft flight envelope.  The  TCAS  complies with  TCAS  II change 7.1 Minimum Operational
Performance Standards ( DO -185B MOPS).  Unless amended in this supplement, all
the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
Buffet Onset
At any flight conditions, it
is possible to determine maneuvering margins before buffet onset occurs,
by reference to the following graphs.
Example 1 (Weight in kg):
For:
M = 0.73
Flight Level
= 350
Weight =  60
CG  =  31
Results: Buffet onset at
M =  0.73  with  54  bank angle or at  1.7
Low Speed ( 1 ): M =  0.54
Example 2 (Weight in lb):
For:
M = 0.73
Flight Level
= 350
Weight =  120000
CG  =  31
Results: Buffet onset at
M =  0.73  with  56  bank angle or at  1.85
Low Speed ( 1 ): M =  0.51
