Normal Landing
The minimum final approach
speed is 1.23  VS1G  of the landing configuration.
Set ENG MODE selector as required.
The flare height should be increased
for landing at high altitude airports particularly with high approach
speeds or for approaches with increased glide slope or
to uphill sloping runways.
Normal Landing
The minimum final approach speed is  VLS  (1.23  VS1G ) of the landing configuration.
Set ENG MODE selector as required.
The flare height should be increased
for landing at high altitude airports particularly with high approach
speeds or for approaches with increased glide slope or to uphill sloping
runways.
If landing with tailwind above  10 :
Before beginning the final descent, it is recommended to stabilize
the speed around  VREF  +  5 .  Decelerated approaches are not allowed.  FLAPS FULL is the recommended configuration for landing.
Normal Landing
The minimum final approach speed is  VLS  (1.23  VS1G ) of the landing configuration.
Set ENG MODE selector as required.
The flare height should be increased
for landing at high altitude airports particularly with high approach
speeds or for approaches with increased glide slope or to uphill sloping
runways.
Normal Landing
The minimum final approach speed is 1.23  VS1G  of the landing
configuration.
Set ENG MODE selector as required.
The flare height should be increased
for landing at high altitude airports particularly with high approach
speeds or for approaches with increased glide slope or to uphill sloping
runways.
If landing with tailwind above  10 :
Before beginning the final descent, it is recommended to stabilize
the speed around  VREF  +  5 .
Decelerated approaches are not
allowed.
If the landing weight is below  60 :
Use FLAPS FULL for
landing.
If the landing weight is at or above  60 :
Use FLAPS FULL or
FLAPS 3 for landing.
Balked Landing (All
Engines Operating)
Apply go-around thrust.
Rotate to achieve a positive
rate of climb and establish the required pitch attitude (not to exceed  18 ) as directed by  SRS  pitch command bar.
Retract slats/flaps one step.
Maintain the final approach
speed.
When a positive rate of climb established:
Retract landing gear.
Balked Landing (All
Engines Operating)
Set the thrust levers to the
TOGA detent to ensure engagement of go-around mode.  Then, set the thrust levers
to FLX/MCT detent to engage the go-around soft mode.
At any time of the procedure, if TOGA thrust is desired :
Set the thrust levers
to TOGA detent.
Rotate the aircraft to achieve
positive rate of climb .  Establish the required pitch
attitude as directed by  SRS  pitch command bar.
Retract slats/flaps one step.
When positive rate of climb is established:
Retract landing gear.
Reverse Thrust
Reverse thrust may be applied
before the nosewheel is on ground (even with one engine inoperative).
Maximum reverse thrust may be applied
down to  70
IAS .
Autobrake
Autobrake may be used provided
the available landing distance is compatible with the performance
of the associated selected automatic mode.
Its use does not relieve the pilot of
the responsibility to achieve a safe stop within the available runway
length, if necessary by taking over brake control with brake pedals.
Disengagement of automatic braking system
may be done either by firm action on the brake pedals or by pressing
the pushbutton of the armed mode.
Operations in Icing
Conditions
For icing conditions definition:  .
When icing conditions are encountered:
Turn on engine anti-ice.
Set wing anti-ice
as required.
If there is evidence of significant ice accretion and to take
into account ice formation on non heated structure:
If CONF FULL:
Minimum
speed:  VLS  +  5
If CONF below FULL:
Minimum
speed:  VLS  +  10
Extended flight
in icing conditions with slats extended should be avoided.
Apply performance
adjustments according to the note of the Approach Climb and Landing
Climb,  .
Operations in Icing
Conditions
For icing
conditions definition:  .
When icing conditions are encountered:
The flight crew
must turn on the engine anti-ice
The flight crew
may turn on the wing anti-ice to prevent ice accretion on the wing
leading edge
The flight crew
must turn on the wing anti-ice if there is evidence of ice accretion,
such as ice on the visual indicators or on the wipers, or if the alert  SEVERE ICE DETECTED  is displayed.
When the aircraft is no longer in icing conditions and the
detection system does not indicate that ice is accreting:
The use of the wing
or engine anti-ice is not required.
For additional information about the
use of engine anti-ice:  .
If there is evidence of significant ice accretion and to take
into account ice formation on non heated structure:
If CONF FULL:
Minimum speed:  VLS  +  5
If CONF below FULL:
Minimum
speed:  VLS  +  10 .
Extended flight
in icing conditions with slats extended should be avoided.
Apply performance
adjustments according to the note of the Approach Climb and Landing
Climb ( ) and Approach
and Landing Speeds (  PERF-LDG Approach and Landing Speeds  ).
General
This supplement is applicable
to dispatch a revenue flight with the landing gear down and the landing
gear doors closed.  Unless amended in this supplement,
all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
Limitations
VMO / MMO  :  235 / 0.6
Flight in forecasted icing conditions is not permitted.
Ditching has not been demonstrated.
Managed vertical modes CLB and DES must not be used.
Managed speed (except in approach) must not be used.
The  FMS  fuel predictions must be disregarded.
Limitations
VMO / MMO  :  235 / 0.6
Flight in forecasted icing conditions is not permitted.
Ditching has not been demonstrated.
Managed vertical modes CLB and DES must not be used.
Managed speed (except in approach) must not be used.
The  FMS  fuel predictions must be disregarded.
The use of go-around soft mode is prohibited.
Preflight Check
The L/G DOWN  VMO / MMO  switch located in the avionic bay on 188VU
must be set on L/G DOWN position.
F/CTL FLIGHT CONTROLS FAILURE
A flight control
failure case normally leading to ALTN LAW will lead to DIRECT LAW.
ENG DUAL FAILURE
If  APU  running:
Perform an assisted
relight
If  APU  not running:
Attempt an  APU  start
If unsuccessful:
Accelerate to  260  to attempt a windmill relight. In that case, disregard  VMO  warning.
Flight controls are in direct law: Use "man pitch trim" as necessary.
In approach:
Set CONF 1 above or
at  200 .
Do not select flaps/slats below  200 .
ALL ENGINES FAILURE
If  APU  running:
Perform an assisted
relight.
If  APU  not running:
Attempt an  APU  start.
If unsuccessful:
Accelerate to  260  to attempt a windmill relight. In that case, disregard  VMO  warning.
Flight controls are in direct law: Use "man pitch trim"
as necessary.
ENG DUAL FAILURE
If  APU  running:
Perform an assisted
relight.
If  APU  not running:
Attempt an  APU  start.
If unsuccessful:
Accelerate to  260  to attempt a windmill relight. In that case, disregard  VMO  warning.
Flight controls are in direct law: Use "man pitch trim"
as necessary.
Rain Repellent (If
Activated)
Use rain
repellent in the case of heavy rain only.
INTRODUCTION
The procedures contained in this chapter
have been established and are recommended by the aircraft manufacturer.
The following important
remarks apply:
These procedures
give information related to system and operational requirements and
cover the actions to be followed in case of failures which are not
considered as emergency cases (these cases are covered in EMERGENCY
PROCEDURES chapter).
Only particular
operations which are considered useful to highlight are presented.
The procedures which are considered to be "basic airmanship"
are therefore not covered.
For a definition
of LAND ASAP, .
When actions depend on a condition, a
dot (•) or a square (■) identifies this condition. The square is used
when there is a choice between one or more conditions and only one
is applicable.  These
procedures are approved by the Airworthiness Authorities as acceptable
procedures for operation of the aircraft. This approval does not prevent
the operator from developing equivalent procedures, provided these
procedures are approved by appropriate Operational Authorities.  In case of discrepancy between procedures
displayed on the  ECAM  and procedures stated in the  AFM , the  AFM  procedures have always precedence.  Unless otherwise specified in the procedures,
the minimum speed to be used for approach and landing is the  VLS  corresponding to the configuration requested by the procedure.
VLS , when mentioned
in a procedure, is the one corresponding to the configuration requested
by the procedure (e.g. if the procedure requests to use FLAPS 2, take  VLS  of CONF 2).
Takeoff Procedure
Turn on both engines  N1  mode.
Set slats, flaps and horizontal
stabilizer as required.
Perform flight controls checks
using the pedals and each sidestick.
Arm ground spoilers and select
maximum autobrake.
Apply  50
N1  on both engines with brakes on.
Brakes may be released so as
to perform a rolling takeoff.
If crosswind at or below  20  and no tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick half forward.
Apply thrust up to  TOGA  thrust whilst maintaining stick half forward up to  80 .
If crosswind above  20  or if tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick full forward.
Apply thrust progressively
to get  TOGA  thrust at  40  ground speed,
whilst maintaining stick close to full forward up to  80 .
Then release stick progressively
to reach neutral at  100 .
The  FADEC  includes
a Keep-Out Zone ( KOZ ) which prevents the engine from being
stabilized between an approximate range of  60  to  74
N1  (limits are a function of the outside
air temperature). Therefore, during engine acceleration on ground,
the pilot may notice a non-linear thrust response to thrust lever
movement.
Check takeoff  N1  is
set prior to reaching  80 .
At  VR :
Rotate the aircraft
with a positive side stick input to achieve a normal and continuous
rotation rate to the pitch attitude necessary to maintain an airspeed
at or above  V2  +  10 .
Once airborne and with a positive rate of climb:
Retract landing gear.
SRS  guidance can be followed when  FD  pitch
order has stabilized.
Disarm ground spoilers.
At safe height:
Accelerate up to at
least 1.18  VS1G  of configuration (1 + F) or "F" speed if
CONF 2 or 3 in use.
Select slats/flaps
position 1 and accelerate up to at least 1.23  VS1G  of clean
configuration ("S" speed).
Select slats/flaps
position 0 and accelerate up to 1.36  VS1G  ("green
dot" speed).
For takeoff
in configuration 1 (1 + F), F speed is not displayed.
If takeoff is
performed with packs off, pack 1 should be selected ON after thrust
reduction to CLB.
Takeoff Procedure
Turn on both engines  N1  mode.
Set slats, flaps and horizontal
stabilizer as required.
Perform flight controls checks
using the pedals and each sidestick.
Arm ground spoilers and select
maximum autobrake.
Apply  50
N1  on both engines with brakes on.
Brakes may be released so as
to perform a rolling takeoff.
If crosswind at or below  20  and no tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick half forward.
Apply thrust up to  TOGA  thrust whilst maintaining stick half forward up to  80 .
If crosswind above  20  or if tailwind:
Release brakes with
stick full forward.
Apply thrust rapidly
to  70
N1 , then progressively to get  TOGA  thrust at  40  ground speed, whilst maintaining stick close
to full forward up to  80 .
Then release stick progressively
to reach neutral at  100 .
The  FADEC  includes
a Keep-Out Zone ( KOZ ) which prevents the engine
from being stabilized between an approximate range of  60  to  74
N1  (limits are a function of the outside
air temperature). Therefore, during engine acceleration on ground,
the pilot may notice a non-linear thrust response to thrust lever
movement.
Check takeoff  N1  is
set prior to reaching  80 .
At  VR :
Rotate the aircraft
with a positive sidestick input to achieve a normal and continuous
rotation rate to the pitch attitude necessary to maintain an airspeed
at or above  V2  +  10 .
Once airborne and with a positive rate of climb:
Retract landing gear.
SRS  guidance can be followed when  FD  pitch
order has stabilized.
Disarm ground spoilers.
At safe height:
Accelerate up to at
least 1.18  VS1G  of configuration (1 + F) or "F" speed if
CONF 2 or 3 in use.
Select slats/flaps
position 1 and accelerate up to at least 1.23  VS1G  of clean
configuration ("S" speed).
Select slats/flaps
position 0 and accelerate up to 1.36  VS1G  ("green
dot" speed).
For takeoff
in configuration 1 (1 + F), F speed is not displayed.
If takeoff is
performed with packs off, pack 1 should be selected ON after thrust
reduction to CLB.
ENG - SHUTDOWN
LAND ASAP
If relevant  FIRE  not pushed:
Turn off affected
side pack.
If wing anti-ice on:
Open crossbleed valve.
Set ENG MODE selector to IGN
START.
If no fuel leak:
Monitor fuel imbalance.
If relevant  FIRE  pushed:
Close crossbleed valve.
Wing anti-ice is inoperative.  .
For go-around, set thrust levers
to  TOGA .

to TOGA.

ENG - REV PRESSURIZED
Set affected engine thrust lever
to idle.
ENG - REVERSE UNLOCKED
If  FADEC  sets affected engine to idle:
LAND ASAP
Set affected engine thrust lever
to idle.
Maximum speed:  300 / 0.78
If buffet:
Reduce speed to  240 .
Shut down affected
engine.
If reversers actually deployed:
Apply full rudder
trim towards live engine.
Control heading with
roll.
For approach and landing:
Use FLAPS 1 for landing.
Turn off  GPWS  flap mode.
Approach speed =  VREF  +  55 .
Set  5  rudder trim towards live engine.
Disconnect autothrust.
Minimize drag until
on glide slope.
At  800
AGL :
Target speed =  VREF  +  40 .
Apply necessary landing
performance corrections.

Performance
For takeoff, en route net flight path
and landing performance determination, the Performance Engineer's
Programs / AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest
approved revision must be used.  .  Select  N1  mode in the SPECIAL CASES field of
the input data for  AFM  performance calculation.
ENG - OIL HI TEMP
Shut down affected engine.
ENG - OIL HI TEMP
Move affected engine thrust
lever to reduce oil temperature below limit.
If overlimit persists after thrust lever is back to idle position:
Shut down the affected
engine.
ENG - FADEC FAULT
On ground, do not set affected
engine thrust lever above idle.
Confirm engine status using
other  ECAM  system pages (ELEC, HYD, BLEED).
If abnormal engine operation:
Shut down affected
engine.
ENG - FADEC FAULT
On ground, do not set affected
engine thrust lever above idle.
Confirm engine status using
other  ECAM  system pages (ELEC, HYD, BLEED).
If abnormal engine operation:
Shut down affected
engine.
ENG - FADEC FAULT
On ground, set affected engine
thrust lever to idle.
Confirm engine status using
other  ECAM  system pages (ELEC, HYD, BLEED).
If abnormal engine operation:
Shut down affected
engine.
ENG -  N1  ( N2 ) ( EGT ) OVERLIMIT
Move affected engine thrust
lever to reduce  N1  ( N2 ) ( EGT ) below
limit.
If overlimit persists after throttle back to idle position:
Shut down the affected
engine.
General
This supplement is applicable to dispatch
the aircraft with both  FADEC s in rated  N1  mode.  Unless amended in this supplement,
all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
ENGINE FAILURE BEFORE V1 (REJECTED TAKEOFF)
Immediately reduce all engine
thrust levers to idle.
Monitor autobrake
operation.
Take over control with brake
pedals if necessary.
If autobrake
is not used, maximum brakes must be applied simultaneously with reduction
of thrust levers.
If takeoff is
rejected above  100 , it is recommended that maximum reverse thrust
is selected.
It is mandatory
to use the maximum reverse thrust when the performance takes benefit
of the reverse thrust effect.
Limitations
Reduced thrust takeoff is not allowed.  Dispatch in degraded  N1  mode
(unrated  N1  mode) is not allowed.
Autothrust is inoperative.
The  N1  value is dependant upon ambient conditions, these may limit
the  N1  to a value lower than that given in LIMITATION chapter
of this  AFM . ( ,  ,  )
ENGINE FAILURE BETWEEN V1 AND V2
If the engine failure occurs before  VR :
Use rudder conventionally
to maintain runway centerline.
At  VR :
Rotate the aircraft
with a positive side stick input to achieve a normal and continuous
rotation rate to a pitch attitude of  12.5 .
Once airborne and with positive rate of climb:
Retract landing gear.
Maintain airspeed
not below  V2 .
SRS  guidance
should be followed when  FD  pitch order is established.
Use rudder to prevent
yaw. Shortly after lift off, the β target will appear. Adjust rudder
position to zero the β target. Control heading conventionally with
bank, keeping β target zeroed with the rudder.
At acceleration height:
Level off.
If aircraft in configuration 2 or 3:
Accelerate up to F
speed and select configuration 1.
Accelerate up to S
speed and select configuration 0.
At slats zero, β target
will disappear: center the sideslip indication conventionally.
Accelerate up to green
dot speed and start climbing at this speed.
Reduce thrust to maximum
continuous (If already in the  MCT / FLX  gate,
move thrust lever to CL and back to  MCT ).
In the case of takeoff performed
with reduced thrust (flexible takeoff), even if the one engine out
takeoff performance is always met with reduced thrust, selection of
full takeoff thrust may be done after engine failure.
ENGINE FAILURE DURING INITIAL CLIMB OUT
Proceed as for takeoff with
engine failure between  V1  and  V2  ( ). However, if failure occurs
above  V2 , it is recommended to maintain the speed reached
after recovery, or  SRS  commanded attitude.
In any case, the speed must not be below  V2 .
APPROACH AND LANDING
Set ENG MODE selector to IGN
START.
In the case of manual landing:
Select the standard
configuration (CONF3 or CONF FULL) for approach and landing.
In the case of automatic landing:
Select CONF FULL
Minimum final approach and landing
speed: 1.23  VS1G  of the landing configuration.
On the  ECAM
F/CTL  page, check that the rudder trim compensates
the aircraft in the correct direction.
If the engine
is inoperative before starting the approach, the minimum  DH  to perform automatic approach, automatic landing and automatic roll
out must be at least  50 .

APPROACH AND LANDING
Set ENG MODE selector to IGN
START.
Select the standard configuration
(CONF3 and CONF FULL) for approach and landing.
Minimum final approach and landing
speed:  VLS  (1.23  VS1G ) of the landing configuration.

before starting the approach, the minimum  DH  to perform
automatic approach, automatic landing and automatic roll out must
be at least  50 .
MISSED APPROACH (FROM INTERMEDIATE APPROACH CONFIGURATION)
Apply go-around thrust.
Rotate the aircraft to a pitch
attitude of  12.5  or as directed by  SRS  pitch command
bar.
Maintain intermediate approach
speed.
Use rudder to prevent yaw. Adjust rudder position to zero the β target. Control heading
conventionally with bank, keeping β target zeroed with the rudder.
At acceleration height:
Level off.
If aircraft in configuration 2 or 3:
Accelerate up to F
speed and select configuration 1.
Accelerate up to S
speed and select configuration 0.
At slats zero, β target
will disappear: center the sideslip indication conventionally.
Accelerate up to green
dot speed and start climbing at this speed.
Reduce thrust to maximum
continuous.
BALKED LANDING
Apply go-around thrust.
Rotate the aircraft to a pitch
of  12.5  or as directed by the  SRS  pitch command
bar.
Retract slats/flaps one step.
Maintain final
approach and landing speed.
When positive rate of climb established:
Retract landing gear.
If necessary, maintain
a speed above the required speed to comply with approach climb gradient.  .
Use rudder to prevent
yaw. Adjust rudder position to zero the β target. Control heading
conventionally with bank, keeping β target zeroed with the rudder.
At acceleration height:
Level off.
If aircraft in configuration 2 or 3:
Accelerate up to F
speed and select configuration 1.
Accelerate up to S
speed and select configuration 0.
At slats zero, β target
will disappear: center the sideslip indication conventionally.
Accelerate up to green
dot speed and start climbing at this speed.
Reduce thrust to maximum
continuous.
BALKED LANDING
Apply go-around thrust.
Rotate the aircraft to a pitch
of  12.5  or as directed by the  SRS  pitch command
bar.
Retract slats/flaps one step.
Maintain final approach and
landing speed.
When positive rate of climb established:
Retract landing gear.
If approach climb performance is not met at 1.23  VS1G  in the usual go-around configuration, the following actions enable
to reach a higher speed:
Select the predefined
airspeed target on  FCU  at or above  150
RA . Accelerate to this new speed target
following  FD  bars in OPEN CLIMB mode.
Use rudder to prevent
yaw. Adjust rudder position to zero the β target. Control heading
conventionally with bank, keeping β target zeroed with the rudder.
At acceleration height:
Level off.
If aircraft in configuration 2 or 3:
Accelerate up to F
speed and select configuration 1.
Accelerate up to S
speed and select configuration 0.
At slats zero, β target
will disappear: center the sideslip indication conventionally.
Accelerate up to green
dot speed and start climbing at this speed.
Reduce thrust to maximum
continuous.
OVERWEIGHT LANDING
If circumstances dictate, landing
may be made at a weight corresponding to the maximum structural takeoff
weight.
If the overweight landing procedure follows a failure requesting
to land in FLAPS 3 or below:
Use the requested
FLAPS setting for landing.
For go-around:
Select FLAPS 1.
Otherwise:
If approach climb performance requirement is met in CONF 3:
Use FLAPS FULL for
landing.
For go-around:
Select FLAPS 3.
If approach climb performance requirement is not met in CONF
3:
Use FLAPS 3 for landing.
For go-around:
Select
FLAPS 1.
At this weight
the maximum touchdown vertical speed should not exceed  360 .
Air conditioning
should be turned off or supplied by  APU .
Approach climb
requirement must be checked.

position 3 for landing.

requirement must be checked.

elevation to  8000 .

to AUTO.

both packs supplied
by  APU  below  17000  or
one single pack
supplied by  APU  bleed below  20000
For takeoff:
If performance requires:
Takeoff can be performed with:
both packs supplied
by  APU  up to  17000  or
one single pack
supplied by  APU  bleed up to  20000

AIR - PACK 1 + 2 FAULT
Turn off both packs.
Descend to the higher one of:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
If only one pack was overheated,
recover the affected pack once overheat has disappeared.
When at or below  100  or  MEA - MORA  and cabin differential pressure below  1 :
Turn on RAM AIR.
AIR - PACK 1 + 2 FAULT
Turn off both packs.
If one pack was previously set to OFF due to a failure of the
associated engine bleed:
Turn on the affected
pack.
In all other cases:
Descend to the higher
one of:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
If only one pack was overheated,
recover the affected pack once overheat has disappeared.
When at or below  100  or  MEA - MORA  and cabin differential pressure below  1 :
Turn on RAM AIR.
CAB PR - SAFETY VALVE OPEN
If cabin differential pressure below  0 :
Expect HI CAB rate.
Reduce aircraft vertical
speed.
If cabin differential pressure above  8 :
Use manual pressurization
mode.
Monitor cabin altitude
and differential pressure are within limits.
If unsuccessful:
Reduce aircraft altitude.
During final approach, maintain
the cabin vertical speed switch in the UP position.
COND - HOT AIR FAULT
Turn off hot air (if not automatically
done).
If unsuccessful:
Turn off both packs.  .
VENT - SKIN VALVE FAULT
Set ventilation blower to OVRD.
Set ventilation extract to OVRD.
If unsuccessful:
Maximum flight level is the
higher one of:  100  or  MEA-MORA .
Use manual pressurization
mode.
Maintain the cabin
vertical speed switch in the UP position.
VENT - SKIN VALVE FAULT
Set ventilation blower to OVRD.
Set ventilation extract to OVRD.
If unsuccessful:
Maximum flight level is the
higher one of:  100  or  MEA-MORA .
Use manual pressurization
mode.
If cabin differential pressure above  1 :
Increase the cabin
vertical speed to target  +1000 .
If cabin differential pressure below  1  or invalid:
Maintain the cabin
vertical speed switch in the UP position.

autobrake and proceed as circumstances dictate.

FUEL IMBALANCE
Compare the  FOB  +  FU  with the  FOB  at departure. If the difference
is significant, or if the  FOB  +  FU  decreases,
suspect a fuel leak.
Do not apply this
procedure if a fuel leak is suspected.  .
Open wing crossfeed valve.
Turn off fuel pumps on the lighter
side and on center tank.
When fuel balanced:
Turn on wing and center fuel pumps.
Close wing crossfeed
valve.
FUEL IMBALANCE
Compare the  FOB  +  FU  with the  FOB  at departure. If the difference
is significant, or if the  FOB  +  FU  decreases,
suspect a fuel leak.
Do not apply this
procedure if a fuel leak is suspected.  .
Open wing crossfeed valve.
Close both center tank transfer
valves.
Turn off fuel pumps on the lighter
side.
When fuel balanced:
Turn on fuel pumps.
Open
both center tank transfer valves.
Close wing crossfeed
valve.

DISPLAY UNIT FAILURE
Affected  DU  blank or display distorted:
Turn off affected  DU  as required.
If  ECAM
DU s affected:
Use  ECAM / ND  SEL.
If  EFIS
DU s affected:
Use  PFD / ND  XFR.
Diagonal line or "INVALID DATA" on affected  DU :
Attempt to recover
affected  DU  by using associated  DMC  switching.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off then on affected  DU .
Inversion of  EWD  and  SD  displays:
Turn off then on  ECAM  upper display.
Affected  DU (s) or  MCDU  flashes intermittently:
If Captain PFD or ND, both ECAM DUs or upper ECAM DU, or MCDU
1 is (are) affected:
Turn off  GEN  1.
If DU(s) stop(s) flashing:
Keep GEN 1 off for
the rest of the flight.  Use the sideslip indication
to verify if the rudder trim needs to be reset. If necessary, reset
the rudder trim.

be started ( ) and  APU  generator may be used (if available).

Restore  GEN  1.

affected:
Turn off
GEN  2.
If DU(s) stop(s) flashing:
Keep GEN 2 off for
the rest of the flight.  Use the sideslip indication
to verify if the rudder trim needs to be reset. If necessary, reset
the rudder trim.

General
This supplement is applicable to aircraft
fitted with Terrain Awareness Warning System ( TAWS ) T2CAS,
or with  TAWS  T3CAS, or with Enhanced Ground Proximity Warning
System ( EGPWS ).  A list
of areas where no terrain data are available along the scheduled route
should be made available to the flight crew.  Approval of  TAWS  predictive functions of T2CAS or T3CAS,
or of EGPWS enhanced function is based on the assumption that  TAWS  databases (excluding Performance database) are compliant
with  DO -200A DPAL2.  Unless
amended in this supplement, all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
Limitations
Aircraft navigation is not to be predicated upon the use of the terrain
display.  The terrain display is intended
to serve as a situation awareness tool only, and may not provide the
accuracy on which to solely base terrain avoidance maneuvering.  The  EGPWS  database, display, and alerting
algorithms currently do not account for man made obstructions.
The  EGPWS  enhanced
function should be inhibited (TERR pushbutton set to OFF on  GPWS  panel) when the aircraft position is less than  15  from the airfield:
For operations
from/to runways not incorporated into the  EGPWS  database
For specific
approach or departure procedures, which have previously been identified
by the operator as potentially causing expected or spurious terrain
alerts.
The decision to inhibit the  EGPWS  enhanced function must not be only based on flight crew
judgement.
Limitations
Aircraft navigation is not to
be predicated upon the use of the terrain display.  The terrain display is intended
to serve as a situation awareness tool only, and may not provide the
accuracy on which to solely base terrain avoidance maneuvering.  The  TAWS  database,
display, and alerting algorithms currently do not account for man
made obstructions.
The  TAWS  predictive
function should be inhibited (TERR pushbutton set to OFF on  GPWS  panel) when the aircraft position is less than  15  from the airfield:
For operations
from/to runways not incorporated into the  TAWS  database
For specific
approach or departure procedures which have previously been identified
by the operator as potentially causing expected or spurious terrain
alerts.
The decision to inhibit
the  TAWS  predictive function must not be only based on
flight crew judgement.

Reverse Thrust
The selection of the thrust reversers
in flight or their preselection before touchdown is prohibited.  On ground, backing the aircraft with use of reverse thrust is not permitted.
Reduced Thrust Takeoff
Takeoff at reduced thrust is allowed
only if the aircraft meets all performance requirements at the takeoff
weight, with the operating engines at the thrust available for the
flex temperature.  Takeoff
at reduced thrust is allowed with any inoperative item affecting the
performance only if the associated performance shortfall has been
applied to meet above requirements.
Allowed inoperative items may
be identified through DISPATCH WITH INOPERATIVE ITEMS chapter of this  AFM  ( ) or through  MMEL .
Takeoff
at reduced thrust is not allowed on contaminated runways.
The flex temperature must not
be:
Higher than  ISA  +  55  (in order that the
amount of thrust reduction does not exceed  25  of the full
rated takeoff thrust)
Lower than the
flat rating temperature or actual  OAT .
Takeoff
at reduced thrust is not allowed unless the operator establishes a
means to verify the availability of takeoff thrust, to ensure that
engine deterioration does not exceed authorized limits.

only if the aircraft meets all performance requirements at the takeoff
weight, with the operating engines at the thrust available for the
flex temperature.  Takeoff

be:

rated takeoff thrust)

flat rating temperature or actual  OAT .

at reduced thrust is not allowed unless the operator establishes a
means to verify the availability of takeoff thrust, to ensure that
engine deterioration does not exceed authorized limits.

Takeoff at reduced thrust is
only permitted, if the airplane meets all applicable performance requirements
at the planned takeoff weight, with the operating engines at the thrust
available for the assumed temperature.  Takeoff at reduced thrust is allowed with any inoperative item affecting
the performance only if the associated performance shortfall has been
applied to meet above requirements.
Allowed inoperative
items may be identified through DISPATCH WITH INOPERATIVE ITEMS chapter
of this  AFM  ( ) or through  MMEL .
Takeoff at reduced thrust is
not allowed on contaminated runways.
The flex temperature must not
be:
Higher than  ISA  + 50
Lower than the
flat rating temperature or actual  OAT .
Takeoff at reduced thrust is
not allowed unless the operator establishes a means to verify the
availability of takeoff thrust, to ensure that engine deterioration
does not exceed authorized limits.
Reduced Thrust Takeoff
Takeoff at reduced thrust is permitted
only if the aircraft meets all performance requirements at the takeoff
weight, with the operating engines at the thrust available for the
flex temperature.  Takeoff
at reduced thrust is permitted with any inoperative item affecting
the performance only if the associated performance shortfall has been
applied to meet above requirements.
Authorized inoperative items
may be identified through DISPATCH WITH INOPERATIVE ITEMS chapter
of the  AFM  ( ) or through  MMEL  ( ).
Takeoff
at reduced thrust is not permitted on contaminated runways.
The flex temperature must not
be:
Above  ISA  +  49
Below the flat
rating temperature (ISA +  15  at
sea level) or actual  OAT .
Takeoff
at reduced thrust is not permitted unless the operator establishes
a means to verify the availability of takeoff thrust, to ensure that
engine deterioration does not exceed authorized limits.
Fuel and Additive Specifications
The fuel system has been certified with:
JET A1, JET B, JP4, JET A, JP5, JP8, RT, TS-1 and N° 3 JET.
See Airbus Consumable Material
List ( CML ) Part 1 / S01-Fuels at the latest issue for approved
fuel specifications.

Fuel Temperature Limits
MINIMUM:
Fuel temperature must not be
less than:
JET A:  -36
JP5:  -42
JET A1, JP8,
N° 3 JET:  -43
JET B:  -46
JP4:  -54
RT, TS-1:  -45
When using JET A:
If  TAT  reaches  -34 , call  ECAM  fuel page and monitor that fuel
temperature remains higher than  -36 .
MAXIMUM:
JET A1, JET
A, JP5, JP8, JET B, JP4, RT, TS-1, N° 3 JET:  +54

values before engine start and if the given flight level is exceeded
before the center tank has been used :

+49  not above  250

Inertial Reference System (IRS)
The  IRS  are compliant with the position accuracy criteria
of  AC  25.4 and  FAR  121 appendix G for a flight
time up to  16  (i.e. in excess of the
aircraft range).  The ground alignment of the  IRS  has been demonstrated to be acceptable between  73  North and  73  South.
If all  ADIRU s have the same magnetic variation table:
Flights using the NAV mode are
prohibited:
North of  73  North, and
South of  60  South.
If one  ADIRU  has a different magnetic variation
table:
Flights using the NAV mode are
prohibited:
North of  60  North, between  30  West and  160  West, and
North of  73  North, and
South of  55  South.
Inertial Reference System (IRS)
The  IRS  are compliant
with the position accuracy criteria of  AC  25-4 and  FAR  121 appendix G for a flight time up to  16  (i.e. in excess of the aircraft range).  The ground alignment of the  IRS  has been demonstrated
to be acceptable between  82  North and  82  South.
If all  ADIRU s have the same magnetic variation table:
Flights using the NAV mode are
prohibited:
North of  73  North, between  90  West and  120  West (magnetic
polar region), and
North of  82  North, and
South of  60  South.
If one  ADIRU  has a different magnetic variation
table:
Flights using the NAV mode are
prohibited:
North of  60  North, between  30  West and  160  West, and
North of  75  North, and
South of  55  South.

Inertial Reference System (IRS)
The  IRS  are compliant
with the position accuracy criteria of  AC  25.4 and  FAR  121 appendix G for a flight time up to  16  (i.e. in excess of the aircraft range).  The ground alignment of the  IRS  has been demonstrated
to be acceptable between  73  North and  73  South.
If all  ADIRU s have the same magnetic variation table:
Flights using the NAV mode are
prohibited:
North of  73  North, and
South of  60  South.
If one  ADIRU  has a different magnetic variation
table:
Flights using the NAV mode are
prohibited:
North of  60  North, between  30  West and  160  West, and
North of  70  North, between  180  West and  160  West, and
North of  73  North, and
South of  55  South.
In-Seat Power Supply System (ISPSS)
The In-Seat Power Supply System ( ISPSS ) for Portable Electronic Devices ( PED  carried
by the passengers) must be turned off during takeoff and landing.
The airworthiness approval of the  ISPSS  for  PED  does not constitute an operational approval to connect a  PED  to the system. Such authorization must be obtained by the operator
from the appropriate authorities.
In-Seat Power Supply System (ISPSS)
The In-Seat Power Supply System
( ISPSS ) for Portable Electronic Devices ( PED  carried by the passengers) must be turned off during takeoff and
landing. The airworthiness approval of the  ISPSS  for  PED  does not constitute an operational approval to connect
a  PED  to the system.
Such authorization must be obtained
by the operator from the appropriate authorities.
In-Seat Power Supply System (ISPSS)
The In-Seat Power Supply System
( ISPSS ) for Portable Electronic Devices ( PED  carried by the passengers) must be turned off during takeoff and
landing. The airworthiness approval of the  ISPSS  for  PED  does not constitute an operational approval to connect
a  PED  to the system.
Compliance with the applicable
airworthiness requirement does not constitute an operational approval.
Such authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.

VA
MAXIMUM DESIGN MANEUVERING SPEED (VA)
This limitation only applies
in alternate or direct flight control laws.
VA
If alternate or direct law is
active:
Full ailerons
and rudder application should be confined to speeds below  VA
Manoeuvres involving
angle of attack near stall should be confined to speeds below  VA .
Rapid and large alternating
control inputs, especially in combination with large changes in pitch,
roll or yaw (e.g. large sideslip angles) may result in structural
failures at any speed, even below VA.

LAND ASAP Definition
LAND ASAP (red)
Land as soon as possible at the nearest
airport at which a safe landing can be made.
LAND ASAP (red)  information is applicable
to a time-critical situation.
LAND ASAP (amber)
Consider landing at the nearest suitable
airport.
The suitability criteria should
be defined in accordance with the Operator's policy.

Performance
For takeoff, en route net flight path
and go-around performance determination the Performance Engineer's
Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved revision
given must be used,  ,
together with the relevant CPDF,  .  Select the LANDING GEARS EXTENDED case in the SPECIAL
CASES field of the input data for AFM performance calculation.
Maneuvers on Ground
During towing,  ±85  of nosewheel
travel must not be exceeded.
Mechanical stop
is designed at  ±95  of nosewheel travel.
Towbarless Operations
Towbarless operations on nose landing
gear (towing and pushback) are approved provided the towbarless towing
operations are performed in compliance with appropriate operational
requirements, using towbarless towing vehicles that are qualified
and operated to preclude damage to the aircraft nosewheel steering
system, or which provide a reliable and unmistakable warning when
damage to the steering system may have occurred. Towbarless towing
vehicles that are specifically accepted for the Airbus A318/A319/A320/A321
aircraft are listed in Airbus WISE In-Service Information ISI 09.11.00001.
Reduced Vertical Separation Minimum (RVSM)
Aircraft have been certified capable
to participate in  RVSM  operations according to  JAA
TGL  6 and  FAA  91- RVSM  requirements.
Compliance with the standard
noted above does not constitute an operational approval. Such authorization
must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate authorities.
AFM Revision
For each revision, a new  LEDU  is issued.
The  LEDU  contains:
The  DU  identification and title
The approval
information ( )
The "M" field that
may provide the following Evolution Code:
The "R"
letter indicates a revised  DU : The content of the  DU  is updated by the revision. A vertical line in the margin
of the  DU  locates the modified part
The "N"
letter indicates a new  DU  introduced by the revision
The "E"
letter indicates an aircraft validity change for the  DU : The list of  MSN s for which the  DU  is effective
has been changed compared to the previous  LEDU , by addition
or deletion of one or several  MSN .
The "T"
field (Temporary Information) that contains a cross if the associated  DU  is a  TDU
Other fields
that contain the list of  MSN s to which the  DU  applies.
The holder of the  AFM  must check that the manual is in accordance with the  LEDU  of the latest approved revision.  The aircraft model envelope  AFM  is continuously updated
with new or revised technical information. A revision of the operator/owner  AFM  is issued based on the latest approved aircraft model envelope  AFM .  In addition, an operator/owner  AFM  revision may be issued following a fleet modification or
an  SB  embodiment.

engine thrust lever to idle.

engine.

decrease temperature.

Shut down affected
engine.

ENG - EPR MODE FAULT
Turn on both engine  N1  mode.
If degraded  N1  mode active:
Align affected engine  N1  on the  N1  engine in rated mode.
Manually adjust thrust.
Autothrust is lost.
Icing Conditions Definition
lcing conditions exist when
the  OAT  on the ground and for takeoff, or when  TAT  in flight, is  10  or below and visible
moisture in any form is present (such as clouds, fog with visibility
of one mile or less, rain, snow, sleet and ice crystals).  Icing conditions also exist on the ground and for takeoff when the  OAT  is  10  or below
when operating on ramps, taxiways, or runways where surface snow,
standing water, or slush may be ingested by the engines or freeze
on engines, nacelles, or engine sensor probes.
Fuel System
Tanks must be emptied
in the following order:
Center tank
Wing tanks.
Inertial Reference
System (IRS)
Ensure  IRS  alignment
is complete and all  IR  are in the NAV mode before the aircraft is moved.
When an  IR  is in the ATT mode:
Magnetic heading will
drift after initialization in a manner similar to a directional gyro
and requires crew monitoring and periodic manual heading updates to
ensure adequate accuracy.
Reduced Vertical
Separation Minimum (RVSM)
The following table gives the
minimum equipment/functions required to begin  RVSM  operation.
Minimum Equipment/Functions Required to begin  RVSM  Operation
Required Equipment/Functions
Quantity
ADR
2
ATC  Transponder
1
Flight Warning Computer
(for Altitude Alert function)
1
Autopilot
1
PFD  function
(for altitude indication)
2
FCU  (for
altitude target selection and OP CLB/OP DES mode engagement)
1
HYD - RSVR LO AIR PR
If pressure fluctuates:
Turn off associated
hydraulic pumps.
If green hydraulic system affected:
Set  PTU  to OFF.
Apply green hydraulic
system low pressure procedure.  .
If yellow hydraulic system affected:
Set  PTU  to OFF.
Apply yellow hydraulic
system low pressure procedure.  .
System may be recovered
at low altitude.
HYD - RSVR OVHT
Turn off associated hydraulic
pumps.
If green hydraulic system affected:
Set  PTU  to OFF.
Apply green hydraulic
system low pressure procedure.  .
If yellow hydraulic system affected:
Set  PTU  to OFF.
Apply yellow hydraulic
system low pressure procedure.  .
System may be recovered if  OVHT  indication has disappeared.
HYD - RSVR LO LVL
Turn off associated hydraulic
pumps.
If green hydraulic system affected:
Set  PTU  to OFF.
Apply green hydraulic
system low pressure procedure.  .
If yellow hydraulic system affected:
Set  PTU  to OFF.
Apply yellow hydraulic
system low pressure procedure.  .
If blue system affected, EMER
GEN is inoperative.
Introduction
The procedures contained in this chapter
have been established and are recommended by the aircraft manufacturer.  Only particular operations which are
considered useful to highlight are presented. The procedures which
are considered to be "basic airmanship" are therefore not
covered.  When actions
depend on a condition, a dot (•) or a square (■) identifies this condition.
The square is used when there is a choice between one or more conditions
and only one is applicable.  These
procedures are approved by the Airworthiness Authorities as acceptable
procedures for a convenient use of the aircraft. This approval does
not prevent the operator from developing equivalent procedures provided
these procedures are approved by appropriate operational authorities.
Takeoff and Landing Noise Levels
Operational approved takeoff (flyover
and lateral) and landing noise levels are also provided for other
Maximum Takeoff Weights  ( MTOW ) and Maximum Landing Weights
( MLW ) given in the LIMITATIONS chapter of this  AFM  ( ).
Airworthiness Standards Compliance
The Flight Management and Guidance
System ( FMGS ) with the associated equipment has been found
to meet the airworthiness requirements and performance criteria of:
JAR  25
ACJ  25.1329 for automatic flight system
JAR
AWO  Subpart 1 - Automatic landing systems
JAR
AWO  Subpart 2 - Category 2 operations
JAR
AWO  Subpart 3 - Category 3 operations.
Compliance with the standards
noted above does not constitute an approval to conduct category II
or III operations. Such authorization must be obtained by the operator
from the appropriate authorities.

supplement is not allowed:

Autoland
Autoland has been demonstrated:
With CAT II
and CAT III  ILS  beam, with  ILS  slope angle inside
a range of  -2.5  and  -3.15
For an airport
that has an elevation between  -1000  and  6500 .
Performance
of ROLL OUT mode has been demonstrated on dry and wet runways.  One autopilot at least must be engaged
in APPR mode and CAT 2 or CAT 3 SINGLE or CAT 3 DUAL capability must
be displayed on  FMA .

phases

CAT II / CAT III Operations
Category II automatic approach without automatic landing
Minimum decision height:  100
One autopilot at least must be engaged in APPR mode and CAT 2 or
CAT 3 SINGLE or CAT 3 DUAL capability must be displayed on  FMA .
Minimum height for  AP  disconnection:  80
Category II automatic approach with automatic landinG
Minimum decision height:  100
One autopilot at least must be engaged in APPR mode and CAT 2 or
CAT 3 SINGLE or CAT 3 DUAL capability must be displayed on  FMA .
Category III fail passive (single) automatic approach and automatic
landing
Minimum decision height:  50
One autopilot at least must be engaged in APPR mode and CAT 3 SINGLE
or CAT 3 DUAL capability must be displayed on  FMA .
Category III fail operational (dual) automatic approach and
automatic landing
Alert height:  100
CAT III with  DH :
Minimum Approach Break-off Height:  25
AGL  based on a demonstrated height loss from when  TOGA  is
selected in a go-around and windspeeds of  5  tail,  15  head or below.
Two autopilots must be engaged in APPR mode and CAT 3 DUAL capability
must be displayed on  FMA .
CAT III without  DH :
Two autopilots must be engaged in APPR mode and CAT 3 DUAL capability
must be displayed on  FMA .
Minimum Runway Visual Range ( RVR ):  75
Maximum wind conditions for CAT II or CAT III automatic approach
or automatic landing and automatic rollout
Headwind:  30
Tailwind:  10
Crosswind:  20

Normal Procedures
landing
Before beginning final descent
it is recommended to stabilize speed around  VREF  +  5 .
Decelerated approaches are not allowed.
FLAPS FULL selection is recommended for landing.
Normal Procedures
landing
Before beginning final descent
it is recommended to stabilize speed around  VREF  +  5 .
Decelerated approaches are not allowed.
Select
FLAPS FULL for landing.
AUTO FLT - FCU 1(2) FAULT
At each baro setting change
on  FCU , crosscheck the validity of both  PFD  altitude
information with the standby altimeter.
AUTO FLT - YAW DAMPER SYS
Turn off then on  FAC  1.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off then on  FAC  2.
Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
AUTO FLT - YAW DAMPER SYS
Turn off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
The
PFD  and  ECAM  will indicate Alternate Law due to the loss of the Yaw
Damper. Nevertheless, the flight controls in pitch and roll remain
in Normal Law, which provide dutch roll damping through ailerons.
Therefore, although ALTN LAW is indicated to the flight crew, the
aircraft does not revert to DIRECT LAW when the landing gear is extended.
For landing the flare
is available as in Normal Law.
AUTO FLT - YAW DAMPER SYS
Turn off then on  FAC  1.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off then on  FAC  2.
The  PFD  and  ECAM  will indicate Alternate Law due to the loss of the Yaw
Damper. Nevertheless, the flight controls in pitch and roll remain
in Normal Law, which provide dutch roll damping through ailerons.
Therefore, although ALTN LAW is indicated to the flight crew, the
aircraft does not revert to DIRECT LAW when the landing gear is extended.
For landing the flare
is available as in Normal Law.
AUTO FLT - FAC 1+2 FAULT
Use rudder with care above  160 .  Turn
off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off both  FAC s.
Flight controls are
in alternate law.  .
AUTO FLT - FAC 1+2 FAULT
Use rudder with care above  140 .  Turn off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off both  FAC s.
Flight controls are
in alternate law.  .
For go-around, set thrust levers
to TOGA.
AUTO FLT - FAC 1+2 FAULT
Use rudder with care above  160 .  Turn
off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off both  FAC s.
The  PFD  and  ECAM  will indicate Alternate Law due to the loss of the Yaw
Damper. Nevertheless, the flight controls in pitch and roll remain
in Normal Law, which provide dutch roll damping through ailerons.
Therefore, although ALTN LAW is indicated to the flight crew, the
aircraft does not revert to DIRECT LAW when the landing gear is extended.
For landing the flare
mode is available as in Normal Law.
AUTO FLT - FAC 1+2 FAULT
Use rudder with care above  140 .  Turn
off then on both  FAC s one after the other.
If unsuccessful:
Turn off both  FAC s.
The  PFD  and  ECAM  will indicate Alternate Law due to the loss of the Yaw
Damper. Nevertheless, the flight controls in pitch and roll remain
in Normal Law, which provide dutch roll damping through ailerons.
Therefore, although ALTN LAW is indicated to the flight crew, the
aircraft does not revert to DIRECT LAW when the landing gear is extended.
For landing the flare
mode is available as in Normal Law.
For go-around, set thrust levers
to  TOGA .
Multiple Failures or Warnings (CAT II)
In case of multiple failures or warnings, the most limiting applies.
Loss of "CAT 2" Capability (CAT II)
If CAT 2 not displayed on  FMA  above  1000 :
Try to recover.
If no recovery:
Revert to CAT I minima.
If CAT 2 disappears on  FMA  between  1000  and  DH :
Go around if visual
references are not sufficient.
If CAT 2 disappears on  FMA  below  DH :
Land if
visual references are sufficient.
Failure Leading to Slats/Flaps less than FLAPS 3 (CAT II)
Revert to CAT I minima.
Disengage the autopilot no later
than  500 .
F/CTL - FLAP LVR NOT ZERO
Set flaps lever to 0.
CAB PR - EXCESS RESIDUAL PR
Turn off both packs.
Alert cabin crew.
SMOKE - FWD or AFT CARGO SMOKE
Turn off affected cargo compartment
isolation valves (as installed).
Turn off cabin fans (if installed).
Discharge affected cargo compartment
agent (as installed).
After landing:
When aircraft stopped:
Disembark
passengers before opening cargo doors.
Antiskid System and/or Nosewheel Steering Failure
(CAT II)
Disconnect the autopilot at
touchdown or when the failure occurs during landing roll.
BRAKES - NORM BRK FAULT
Apply necessary landing performance
corrections.
BRAKES - NORM + ALTN FAULT
Only parking brake is available.
Antiskid is inoperative.  .
BRAKES - RELEASED
Apply necessary landing performance
corrections.
Alpha Floor Activation (CAT II)
If activation occurs above  1000 :
Check the speed.
Disconnect and reengage the autothrust mode (TOGA LK should disappear from the  FMA ).
If activation occurs below  1000 :
Go around if visual references are not sufficient.
Alpha floor protection is inhibited
below  100  at landing.

and rollout have been demonstrated with one engine inoperative

thrust.

side engine bleed.

AIR - ENG 1(2) BLEED LO TEMP
Disconnect autothrust.
Increase affected engine thrust.
If unsuccessful:
If opposite bleed available:
Open crossbleed valve.
Turn off affected
side engine bleed.
Turn off affected
side pack (if the other pack is on).
If opposite bleed not available:
Turn off wing anti-ice.  .
AIR - ENG 1 + 2 BLEED LO TEMP
Disconnect autothrust.
Increase engines thrust.
If unsuccessful, wing anti-ice
is inoperative.  .

master lever to OFF.

flow on affected side.

Start  APU  as required.

Turn off affected  IDG .

FAULT

affected  ADR s.

of appropriate affected  ADR s.

unreliable airspeed procedure.  .

General
This chapter of the  AFM  gives
the performance information for operations on contaminated runways
and the conditions used for their establishment.
Any actual condition different from those listed in
this chapter may lead to different performance.  .  This chapter
of the  AFM  is approved by  EASA  according to  JAR  25 contaminated runway performance requirements.  This chapter
is not required by  FAR  25 and is provided for guidance
only for those aircraft certificated according to this
requirement.
Limitations
Reduced thrust takeoff is not allowed on contaminated runways.
Dispatch from and to a runway covered
with wet ice is not permitted, unless an approval has been obtained
from the appropriate authorities for a method for the assessment of
the runway condition and corresponding computation of performance.
Introduction
The considered atmosphere is
the international standard atmosphere.  Performance
are related to  VS1G .
Wind speed is measured at the
height of  10 .
Takeoff and Landing Performance
For takeoff and landing performance
determination on contaminated runways, the Performance Engineer's
Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the latest approved revision
must be used.  .
Aircraft Configuration
Takeoff on contaminated runway
The takeoff performance has
been established for slats/flaps configuration 1, 2 and 3 on runways
contaminated by:
6  and  13  of water
6  and  13  of slush
10  and  100  of dry snow
5 ,  15 , and  30  of wet snow
Compacted snow
Ice (cold and
dry).
Ground
spoilers are armed.
Accelerate Stop Distance ( ASD ) determination is made with or without thrust reversers
and considering the use of ground spoilers and wheel brakes with anti-skid
on.
Landing distance on very low braking friction surface
Landing distance on very low braking
friction surface such as icy runway has been established for slats/flaps
configuration 3 and FULL.  Ground spoilers are armed.
Landing distance determination is made with or without
thrust reversers and considering the use of ground spoilers and wheel
brakes with anti-skid on.
AP/FD, Speed modes,
Autothrust
All  AP / FD  modes may be used with or without autothrust except if specified.
Autothrust
may be used with or without  AP / FD , in selected
or managed speed/Mach.
Takeoff
Autopilot
engagement is approved with use of  SRS  + (HDG, TRK, RWY,
RWY TRK or NAV) at or above  100
AGL  and at least  5  after liftoff.
At acceleration
altitude, the use of CLB or OP CLB or V/S or  FPA  or ALT*
or ALT mode is approved.
Use of  FD  is approved in the same modes after the rotation.
Climb, Cruise, Descent
The use of  AP  or  FD  in the following modes is approved:
Lateral modes:
HDG, TRK, NAV
Vertical modes:
V/S,  FPA , ALT*, ALT, ALT CRZ, ALT CST, OP
CLB, EXP CLB, OP DES, EXP DES, CLB, DES.
Climb, Cruise, Descent
The use of  AP  or  FD  in the following modes is approved:
Lateral modes:
HDG, TRK, NAV
Vertical modes:
V/S,  FPA , ALT*, ALT, ALT CRZ, ALT CST, OP
CLB, OP DES, CLB, DES.
Non Precision Approach
The following modes are approved
to be used with  AP  and/or  FD :
Lateral modes:
LOC, HDG, TRK, NAV, APP NAV
Vertical modes:
ALT*, ALT, V/S,  FPA , FINAL APP.
Non Precision Approach
The use of  AP  or  FD  in the following modes is approved:
Lateral modes:
HDG, TRK, NAV
Vertical modes:
V/S,  FPA , ALT*, ALT, ALT CRZ*, ALT CRZ, ALT CST*,ALT CST,
OP CLB, OP DES, CLB, DES, TCAS.
Non Precision Approach
The following modes are approved
to be used with  AP  and/or  FD :
Lateral modes:
F-LOC*, F-LOC,  LOC *,  LOC ,  LOC-BC *,  LOC-BC , HDG, TRK, NAV, APP NAV
Vertical modes:
F-G/S*, F-G/S, ALT*, ALT, V/S,  FPA , FINAL APP, TCAS.
CAT I ILS Approach
The use of  AP  and/or  FD  with or without autothrust is approved in APPR modes (GS*,
LOC*, GS, LOC, LAND).
Dual  AP  engagement
is approved.
CAT II ILS Approach
The use
of  AP  with or without  FD  with
or without autothrust is approved in APPR modes (GS*, LOC*, GS, LOC,
LAND).  Dual  AP  engagement is approved.  One engine may be inoperative.
Compliance with CAT II approach
criteria has been demonstrated with CAT II or CAT III performance
quality  ILS  beam only.
CAT II/III ILS Approach
and Automatic Approach
The use
of  AP  with or without  FD  with autothrust for
CAT III and with or without autothrust for CAT II is approved in APPR
modes (GS*, LOC*, GS, LOC, LAND, FLARE, ROLL OUT) in CONF FULL and
CONF 3.  Dual  AP  engagement is approved.
CAT III Dual
approach is not available with one engine inoperative.
Compliance with
CAT II and CAT III approach and landing criteria has been demonstrated
with CAT II and CAT III performance quality  ILS  beam
only.
Go-around
The use
of  AP  and/or  FD  is approved with use of  SRS  + (GA TRK, HDG, TRK or NAV) modes.
At acceleration
altitude, the use of CLB or OP CLB or V/S or  FPA  or ALT* or ALT mode is approved.

CAT II and CAT III
Approach and/or Automatic Landing
Before the approach:
Select  DH  (or «NO»  DH  if applicable).
For automatic landing
in CAT I or better weather condition, select appropriate  MDA .
During interception of final approach:
Arm APPR mode.
Check approach phase
is active on  MCDU  PERF page.
Engage second autopilot
if available.
Engage autothrust
(not required for CAT II).
Approach speed is  VLS  + wind correction (minimum wind correction:  5 , maximum wind correction:  15 ).
Check desired capability
on  PFD .
If APPR is selected at high
altitude, CAT 1 only will be displayed initially on  FMA . The valid capability will be displayed at lower altitude.
During final approach and landing:
At  350
RA , check ″LAND″ is displayed on  PFD  and  ILS / MLS  course on heading scale.
At  30 , check ”FLARE” is displayed on  PFD .
At  10 , reduce thrust to idle.
At touchdown, check
”ROLL OUT” is displayed on  PFD .
At the latest when
leaving the runway, disconnect the autopilot.
For CAT II automatic
approach, the autopilot should be disconnected at or above  80  if manual landing is intended.
For CAT III
A automatic approach and landing, the autopilot may be disconnected
at touchdown if external visual references are sufficient.
A callout (indicating that a
flight parameter is exceeded) must be made if:
Speed goes below  VAPP  -  5  or above  VAPP  +  10
Pitch attitude
goes below  -2.5  or above  +10  nose up
Bank angle goes
above  7
Descent rate
goes above  1000
Excessive  LOC  or GLIDE deviation occurs.
For CAT II approach
climb performance, refer to  FCOM  In Flight Performance
Chapter.
Whenever the
required landing distance for automatic landing is higher than the
required landing distance for manual landing, corrections to be added
to the required landing distance for manual landing (provided by the
Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the
latest approved revision reported in Performance chapter of this  AFM ) are given in the Automatic Landing Distance Increment
chapter of this  AFM
. The required landing distance for automatic landing is calculated
with the same braking conditions as for manual landing (i.e. full
pilot braking at main landing gear touchdown) but with a lower multiplicative
coefficient (1.15 instead of 1.66).
CAT II and CAT III
Approach and/or Automatic Landing
Before the approach:
Select  DH  (or «NO»  DH  if applicable).
For automatic landing
in CAT I or better weather condition, select appropriate  MDA .
During interception of final approach:
Arm APPR mode.
Check approach phase
is active on  MCDU  PERF page.
Engage second autopilot
if available.
Engage autothrust
(not required for CAT II).
Approach speed is  VLS  + wind correction (minimum wind correction:  5 , maximum wind correction:  15 ).
Check desired capability
on  PFD .
If APPR is selected at high
altitude, CAT 1 only will be displayed initially on  FMA . The valid capability will be displayed at lower altitude.
During final approach and landing:
At  350
RA , check ″LAND″ is displayed on  PFD  and  ILS / MLS  course on heading scale.
At  30 , check ”FLARE” is displayed on  PFD .
At  10 , reduce thrust to idle.
At touchdown, check
”ROLL OUT” is displayed on  PFD .
At the latest when
leaving the runway, disconnect the autopilot.
For CAT II automatic
approach, the autopilot should be disconnected at or above  80  if manual landing is intended.
For CAT III
A automatic approach and landing, the autopilot may be disconnected
at touchdown if external visual references are sufficient.
For automatic
landing in tailwind conditions, it is recommended to use manual braking
without delay or automatic braking for an optimized runway centerline
tracking during automatic rollout.
A callout (indicating that a
flight parameter is exceeded) must be made if:
Speed goes below  VAPP  -  5  or above  VAPP  +  10
Pitch attitude
goes below  -2.5  or above  +10  nose up
Bank angle goes
above  7
Descent rate
goes above  1000
Excessive  LOC  or GLIDE deviation occurs.
For CAT II approach
climb performance, refer to  FCOM  In Flight Performance
Chapter.
Whenever the
required landing distance for automatic landing is higher than the
required landing distance for manual landing, corrections to be added
to the required landing distance for manual landing (provided by the
Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the
latest approved revision reported in Performance chapter of this  AFM ) are given in the Automatic Landing Distance Increment
chapter of this  AFM
. The required landing distance for automatic landing is calculated
with the same braking conditions as for manual landing (i.e. full
pilot braking at main landing gear touchdown) but with a lower multiplicative
coefficient (1.15 instead of 1.66).

law.  .

Set  ATC /XPDR to SYS
1.
Turn off  ADR  2 and  ADR  3.
For go-around, set thrust levers
to  TOGA .

Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
Set AIR DATA switching to NORM.
Set  ATC /XPDR to SYS
1.
Turn off  ADR  2 and  ADR  3.
For go-around, set thrust levers
to TOGA.
NAV - ADR 2+3 FAULT
Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
If  ADR  1 reliable:
Set AIR DATA switching
to NORM.
Turn on BKUP SPD/ALT
on  F/O
PFD
Set ATC/XPDR to SYS
1.
If  ADR  1 not reliable:
Turn on BKUP SPD/ALT
on both  PFD s
Maximum speed:  280  /  0.78
Turn off  ADR  2 and  ADR  3.
For go-around, set thrust levers
to TOGA.
NAV - ADR 2+3 FAULT
Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
Set AIR DATA switching to NORM.
Set  ATC /XPDR to SYS
1.
Turn off  ADR  2 and  ADR  3.
If CAPT  AOA  probe heater fault:
Avoid icing conditions.
For go-around, set thrust levers
to TOGA.
General
The  FMGS  lateral and vertical
navigation has been certified for after takeoff, en route and terminal
area operations, and for instrument approach procedures (except  ILS ,  LOC ,  LOC-BC ,  LDA ,  SDF ,  GLS  and  MLS ) and missed approach
procedures.  Approval
of the  FMGS  is based on the assumption that the navigation
database has been validated for intended use. The airworthiness approval
does not account for database accuracy or compatibility.
Obstacle clearance and adherence to airspace
constraints remains a flight crew responsibility.  Fuel, time predictions/performance information
is provided for advisory purpose only.  For instrument procedures not coded in
the WGS-84 coordinate system, the  GPS  must be deselected,
unless the shift between the local coordinate system and the WGS-84
is found acceptable for the intended operation.
The assessment of this shift
can be done:
In flight, monitoring
the navaid raw data in non  RNAV  procedures
On ground, performing
a  GPS  survey of the procedure waypoints.
RNAV  ( GPS ) and  RNP
RNAV  approach procedures
require WGS-84 coordinates and  GPS  PRIMARY.
General
The  FMGS  lateral
and vertical navigation has been certified for after takeoff, en route
and terminal area operations, and for instrument approach procedures
(except  ILS ,  LOC ,  LOC-BC ,  LDA ,  SDF ,  GLS  and  MLS ) and missed approach
procedures.  The  FLS  ( FMS  Landing System) function has been certified for instrument approach
procedures using  FMS  navigation alone or  FMS  navigation associated with  LOC , or  ILS  ( G/S  out), or  LOC-BC
The  FLS  is not certified for  ILS ,  MLS ,  GLS ,  LDA ,  SDF  approaches or  RNAV ( GNSS ) final approaches with  LPV  minimum.  Approval of the  FMGS  is based on the assumption that the
navigation database has been validated for intended use. The airworthiness
approval does not account for database accuracy or compatibility  Obstacle clearance and adherence to airspace constraints remains
a flight crew responsibility.  Fuel, time predictions/performance
information is provided for advisory purpose only.  For instrument procedures not coded in the WGS-84 coordinate system,
the  GPS  must be deselected, unless the shift between the
local coordinate system and the WGS-84 is found acceptable for the
intended operation.
The assessment of this shift
can be done:
In flight, monitoring
the navaid raw data in non  RNAV  procedures
On ground, performing
a  GPS  survey of the procedure waypoints.
RNAV  ( GPS ) and  RNP
RNAV  approach procedures
require WGS-84 coordinates and  GPS  PRIMARY.
Airworthiness Standards Compliance
The  FMGS  has been
demonstrated to comply with applicable airworthiness requirements,
including  FAA
AC  20-130A, for a navigation system
integrating multiple navigation sensors, when operating with aircraft
position based on:
IRS  position and  GPS  update, or
IRS  position and radio navaid update, or
IRS  position only.
The  FMGS  also complies
with the airworthiness part of:
EASA
AMC  20-4 (or  JAA
TGL  2 Rev 1) for
Basic  RNAV
EASA
AMC  20-27 for  RNP  APPROACH ( RNP  APCH) Operations with or without APV BARO- VNAV  Operations
FAA  Advisory Circular 90-105A for RNP2 operations in domestic, oceanic
and remote continental area
FAA  AC 90-105 for:
RNP1 operations
in Terminal area with or without RF leg
RNP
APCH  operations with or without APV BARO-VNAV Operations
in final approach segment
RNP
APCH  operations with or without RF leg capability in
the initial, the intermediate and the missed approach segments.
RNP  APCH without APV BARO- VNAV  operation corresponds to  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach with  LNAV  Minimum.
RNP  APCH with APV BARO- VNAV  operation corresponds to  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach with  LNAV / VNAV  Minimum.
JAA
TGL  10 for Precision  RNAV  (compliance with
paragraph 8.2 has not been demonstrated)
FAA  Advisory Circular 20-129 for baro  VNAV
FAA  Advisory Circular 90-100A for terminal and en route  RNAV  operations
FAA  Order 8400.33 for  RNP  4 in oceanic and remote area
FAA  Order 8400.12A for  RNP  10 in oceanic and remote area.
RNP  10 oceanic/remote
area operations are approved:
With  GPS  PRIMARY
Without  GPS  PRIMARY ( GPS  deselected or inoperative), provided
time limitations in  IRS  only navigation, acceptable to
the operational authorities, are established.
Compliance with the applicable
airworthiness requirements does not constitute an operational approval.
Such authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.

For  RNP
AR  ( SAAAR  or equivalent):
The aircraft is compliant with the airworthiness part of the  EASA
AMC  20-26 and  FAA
AC  90-101A
Appendix 2 and  FAA
AC  20-138C Appendix 2.
Compliance with the
applicable airworthiness requirements does not constitute an operational
approval. Such authorization must be obtained by the operator from
the appropriate authorities.
The aircraft is capable of conducting  RNP
AR  operations when operated in accordance with the recommendations
provided in the Airbus Airworthiness Compliance Document ( ACD ) reference SA34D15033461 issue 4 or higher, Flight Crew Operating
Manual (FCOM) and bulletins.  Detailed  RNP
AR  levels approved through the
Airworthiness Approval of the aircraft are provided in the  ACD .  Guidance for Operational Approval is provided in the  ACD .
RNP
AR  performance has been demonstrated with  AP  ON  (See the
note below), based on the operational assumptions of the  ACD  for the following modes:
Departure in
NAV mode,
Initial approach
in NAV or APP NAV modes,
Final approach
in FINAL APP mode,
Missed approach
in NAV mode.
Navigation performance and recommendations
regarding  RNP
AR  operations with  AP  OFF /  FD  ON are available in the  ACD .
Without  GPS  PRIMARY:
The  FMGS  is certified in accordance with the accuracy
requirements and assumptions of  MASPS
ED -75/ DO -236 for  RNP  operations provided the appropriate  RNP  value is checked or entered on the  MCDU  and
HIGH accuracy is displayed.  The navigation accuracy (without  GPS  PRIMARY) is a function
of ground radio navaid infrastructure or elapsed time since last radio
update.
Use of NAV Mode
NAV mode may be used:
After takeoff provided:
GPS  PRIMARY is available, or
FMGS  takeoff updating has been checked.
In terminal area provided:
GPS  PRIMARY is available, or
HIGH accuracy
is displayed and the appropriate  RNP  is checked or entered
on the  MCDU , or
FMS navigation
is cross-checked with Navaid raw data.
NAV, or NAV and APP NAV and
FINAL APP mode may be used for  VOR ,  VOR / DME ,  NDB ,  NDB / DME  or  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach but not for  ILS ,  LOC ,  LOC - BC ,  LDA ,  SDF ,  GLS  or  MLS  final approach.
Use of NAV Mode and FLS
Use of NAV mode

GPS  PRIMARY is available, or
FMGS  takeoff updating has been checked.
In terminal area provided:
GPS  PRIMARY is available, or
HIGH accuracy
is displayed and the appropriate  RNP  is checked or entered
on the  MCDU , or

is cross-checked with Navaid raw data.
NAV, or

Use of FLS
The  FLS  may be used:
For a VOR, VOR/DME,
NDB or NDB/DME approach provided F-APP or F-APP + RAW capability is
displayed on  FMA , or
To provide baro-VNAV
guidance for an  ILS  ( G/S  out), LOC, LOC-B/C
approach in association with LOC or LOC-B/C modes provided F-APP or
F-APP + RAW capability is displayed on  FMA , or
For an  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach with LNAV/VNAV Minimum or with
LNAV Minimum provided F-APP capability is displayed on  FMA .
Approaches
VOR ,  VOR / DME ,  NDB  or  NDB / DME  approach
procedures may be performed, in NAV, or NAV and APP NAV and FINAL
APP mode, provided  AP  or  FD  is used and:
GPS  PRIMARY is available. In this case, the reference navaid may be
unserviceable, or the airborne radio equipment may be inoperative,
or not installed, provided an operational approval is obtained.
GPS  PRIMARY is not available. In this case, the reference navaid and
the corresponding airborne equipment are serviceable, tuned and monitored
during the approach.
RNAV ( GNSS ) approaches may be performed, in NAV, or NAV and APP NAV and FINAL
APP mode provided GPS PRIMARY is available and with  AP  or  FD  engaged.
RNAV ( GNSS ) approaches with  LNAV / VNAV  Minimum must be
performed in FINAL APP mode.
The  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach limitations and procedures must be used to fly a  RNAV  approach where the  GPS  is not required.

Conventional NPA with radio navaid

may be flown using the  FLS  provided:

is displayed on  FMA .  In this case, the

is obtained.
F-APP + RAW
capability is displayed on  FMA .  In this case, the

A  VOR ,  VOR / DME ,  NDB  or  NDB / DME  approach
may be flown in NAV, or NAV and APP NAV and FINAL APP mode provided:
GPS  PRIMARY is available.  In this case, the

may be inoperative, or not installed, provided an operational approval

GPS  PRIMARY is not available  In this case, the

An  ILS  ( G/S  out),  LOC , LOC-B/C approach may be flown using the F-G/S
part of  FLS  function in association with  LOC  or LOC-B/C modes provided:
F-APP capability
is displayed on  FMA .  In this case, the
reference navaid used for vertical path validation must be tuned and
checked at final descent point.
F-APP + RAW
capability is displayed on  FMA .  In this case, the
reference navaid used for vertical path validation must be tuned,

General
The final approach ( FAF  or  FAP  to runway or  MAP ), as extracted from
the navigation database and inserted in the primary  F-PLN  including altitude constraints, must not be modified by the crew.
Before starting the approach,
the crew must check the lateral and vertical profile of the  FMS  approach against the published approach chart.
Instrument Approach Using NAV Mode
For approach procedure with  A/C  in  GPS  PRIMARY
If  GPS  PRIMARY LOST indication appears on  ND  during the approach:
Discontinue the approach, unless:
GPS  PRIMARY is lost on only one  FMGC , the approach can be
continued using  AP / FD  associated to the other  FMGC .
GPS  is not required and navigation accuracy is confirmed against the
radio navaid raw data, or
If  FM / GPS  POS DISAGREE  ECAM  caution is triggered during the approach:
Discontinue the approach
unless radio navaid raw data is available and indicates correct navigation
to continue the approach using selected  FMGS  modes.
For  RNAV ( GNSS ) approaches with  LNAV / VNAV  Minimum:
Between the  FAP  and the  DA , discontinue the approach as soon as the deviation
below the vertical path exceed  75  unless external
visual references are sufficient.
For approach procedure without  GPS  PRIMARY
Before starting the
approach, check  FM  position accuracy with radio navaid
raw data.
If HIGH accuracy is lost during a  VOR ,  VOR / DME ,  NDB  or  NDB / DME  instrument
approach procedure:
The approach can be
continued in NAV mode if the navigation accuracy is confirmed against
the radio navaid raw data.

For dispatch conditions:
Windshear Warning
and Guidance System
Windshear detection is available
below  1300  at takeoff, approach and go-around.
In the
case of warning,  TOGA  power application provides guidance
in SRS mode, using  FD  or  AP  .
If engaged  AP  may be used for go-around.
FD  guidance may lead to speeds of  VALPHAMAX  (top of the red strip along the speed scale of the  PFD ).
The aircraft configuration should not be changed before end
of windshear conditions.
Altitude Loss with Autopilot Malfunction (CAT II)
Demonstrated Altitude Loss Below Glide Slope with Autopilot
Malfunction
In approach
one  AP  engaged in APPR mode, with take over  1  after failure recognition, the path deviation is negligible.
LOSS OF BRAKING
If autobrake selected:
Take over brake control
with brake pedals.
If no braking available:
Apply maximum reverse
thrust.
Release brake pedals.
Turn off antiskid.
Press brake pedals.
Apply maximum brake
pressure  1000 .
If still no braking:
Use short and successive
applications of the parking brake.

76 and 77.

disconnection:  160
AGL .

be displayed on  FMA .

approach OR automatic landing

to maximum wind conditions for CAT II operations ( ).
Multiple Failures or Warnings (GLS)
In case of multiple
failures or warnings, the most limiting applies.
Altitude Loss with Autopilot Malfunction (GLS)
Demonstrated Altitude Loss Below Glide Slope with Autopilot
Malfunction
In approach one  AP  engaged in APPR mode, with take over  1  after failure recognition, the path deviation is negligible.
Failure Leading to Slats/Flaps less than FLAPS 3 (GLS)
Disengage the autopilot
no later than  500 .
Antiskid System and/or Nosewheel Steering Failure (GLS)
Disconnect the
autopilot at touchdown or when the failure occurs during landing roll.
Alpha Floor Activation (GLS)
If activation occurs above  1000 :
Check the speed.
Disconnect and reengage
the autothrust mode (TOGA LK should disappear from the  FMA ).
If activation occurs below  1000 :
Go around if visual references are not sufficient.
Alpha floor protection is inhibited
below  100  at landing.
Alpha Floor Activation (GLS)
If activation occurs above  1000 :
Check the speed.
If activation occurs below  1000 :
Go around if visual
references are not sufficient.
Alpha floor protection is inhibited
below  100  at landing.

and rollout have been demonstrated with one engine inoperative before
initiating the approach.

Apply the standard
One Engine Inoperative procedure. .

Go around if visual
references are not sufficient.
If failure occurs below  DH :
Land
if external visual references are sufficient.

CAT I GLS Approach
The use of  AP  and/or  FD  with or without autothrust is approved in APPR modes (G/S*,
LOC*, G/S, LOC, LAND).  Dual  AP  engagement is approved.
CAT I GLS Approach and Automatic Landing
The use of  AP  with
or without  FD  with or without autothrust is approved in
APPR modes (G/S*, LOC*, G/S, LOC, LAND, FLARE, ROLL OUT) in configuration
3 and FULL.  Dual  AP  engagement
is approved.
GLS CAT I Approach
and/or Automatic Landing
Before the approach:
Select  DA / DH  (BARO).
During interception of final approach:
Arm APPR mode.
Check approach phase
is active on  MCDU  PERF page.
Engage second autopilot
if available.
Engage autothrust
if available.
Approach speed is  VLS  + wind correction (minimum wind correction:  5 , maximum wind correction:  15 ).
Check desired capability
on  PFD .
During final approach and landing:
At  350
RA , check ″LAND″ is displayed on  PFD  and  GLS  course on heading scale.
At  30 , check ”FLARE” is displayed on  PFD .
At  10 , reduce thrust to idle.
At touchdown, check
”ROLL OUT” is displayed on  PFD .
At the latest when
leaving the runway, disconnect the autopilot.
For  GLS  CAT I automatic approach, the autopilot should be disconnected at
or above  160  if manual landing is intended.
A callout (indicating that a
flight parameter is exceeded) must be made if:
Speed goes below  VAPP  -  5  or above  VAPP  +  10
Pitch attitude
goes below  -2.5  or above  +10  nose up
Bank angle goes
above  7
Descent rate
goes above  1000
Excessive  LOC  or GLIDE deviation occurs.
Whenever the
required landing distance for automatic landing is higher than the
required landing distance for manual landing, corrections to be added
to the required landing distance for manual landing (provided by the
Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module at the
latest approved revision reported in Performance chapter
of this  AFM ) are given in the Automatic Landing Distance
Increment chapter of this  AFM
. The required landing distance for automatic
landing is calculated with the same braking conditions as for manual
landing (i.e. full pilot braking at main landing gear touchdown) but
with a lower multiplicative coefficient (1.15 instead of 1.66).
GLS CAT I Approach and/or Automatic Landing
Before the approach:
Select  DA / DH  (BARO).
During interception of final approach:
Arm APPR mode.
Check approach phase
is active on  MCDU  PERF page.

if available.

if available.

on  PFD .

RA , check ″LAND″ is displayed on  PFD  and  GLS  course on heading scale.

At  10 , reduce thrust to idle.
At touchdown, check
”ROLL OUT” is displayed on  PFD .
At the latest when
leaving the runway, disconnect the autopilot.
For  GLS  CAT I automatic approach, the autopilot should be disconnected at
or above  160  if manual landing is intended.
For automatic
landing in tailwind conditions, it is recommended to use manual braking
without delay or automatic braking for an optimized runway centerline
tracking during automatic rollout.

flight parameter is exceeded) must be made if:
Speed goes below  VAPP  -  5  or above  VAPP  +  10
Pitch attitude
goes below  -2.5  or above  +10  nose up
Bank angle goes
above  7

goes above  1000

the latest approved revision reported in Performance chapter of this  AFM ) are given in the Automatic Landing Distance Increment
chapter of this  AFM
. The required landing distance for automatic landing is calculated
