Instrument Approach using FLS
Conventional NPA with radio navaid
The following procedure applies
to:
VOR ,  VOR / DME ,  NDB ,  NDB / DME  approach, or
Baro-VNAV guidance
for  ILS  ( G/S  out),  LOC ,  LOC - B/C  approach (in association with  LOC  or  LOC - B/C  modes).
Before starting the approach:
Verify that  FLS  beam
is correctly located and oriented using  PFD  and  ND  data to check:
The approach
course, the approach vertical flight path angle and the  FLS  beam anchor point location
The  FLS  beam compatibility with the underlying  FMS
F-PLN .
Check that  QNH  baro setting is selected.
Obstacle clearance is not guaranteed:
If erroneous
baro setting is selected, or
If  FLS  guidance is used outside the published approach.
Check that the TEMP
is entered in the MCDU FMS PERF APP page to ensure cold weather correction
if necessary.
Check the approach
capability is F-APP or F-APP + RAW on the  FMA.
During the approach:
Check the final descent
point with a means appropriate for the approach.
If GPS PRIMARY LOST indication appears on one  ND :
Continue the approach
using the  FLS  data associated with the  FMS  for
which GPS PRIMARY is still available.
If F-APP capability is lost on  FMA  with F-APP +
RAW displayed on  FMA :
Continue the approach
using the  FLS  data provided the reference navaid is monitored
and indicates correct navigation.
If F-APP or F-APP + RAW capability is lost with RAW ONLY displayed
on  FMA :
Continue the approach
in selected vertical and lateral AP/FD modes or without AP/FD.
LS  data
must be deselected and appropriate ND display must be used for navigation
with the reference navaid raw data.
Below Minimums:
Use outside visual
observation as primary reference.
FLS  deviations
can be used for advisory purpose when approach axis is aligned with
the runway.
RNAV(GNSS) Approach
The following procedure applies
to an  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach:
With  LNAV  Minimum, or
With  LNAV / VNAV  Minimum.
Before starting the approach:
Verify that  FLS  beam
is correctly located and oriented using  PFD  and  ND  data to check:
The approach
course, the approach vertical flight path angle and the  FLS  beam anchor point location.
The  FLS  beam compatibility with the underlying FMS F-PLN.
Check that QNH baro
setting is selected.
Obstacle clearance is not guaranteed:
If erroneous
baro setting is selected, or
If  FLS  guidance is used outside the published approach.
Check that the TEMP
is entered in the MCDU FMS PERF APP page to ensure cold weather correction
if necessary.
Check that approach
capability is F-APP on  FMA .
During the approach:
Check the final descent
point with a means appropriate for the approach.
If GPS PRIMARY LOST indication appears on one  ND :
Continue the approach
using the  FLS  data associated with the  FMS  for
which GPS PRIMARY is still available.
If F-APP capability is lost and F-APP + RAW or RAW only is
displayed on  FMA :
Discontinue the approach.
Between the  FAP  and Minimums:
For an  RNAV ( GNSS ) approach with  LNAV / VNAV  Minimum:
Discontinue the approach
as soon as the F-G/S deviation exceeds half a dot below the F-G/S
beam unless external visual references are sufficient.
Below Minimums:
Use outside visual
observation as primary reference.
FLS  deviations
can be used for advisory purpose when approach axis is aligned with
the runway.
Tire Speed
Maximum tire speed:  195  (ground speed).
STALL RECOVERY
Apply nose down pitch control.
Use lateral control to level
wings.
When out of stall:
Smoothly increase
thrust as needed.
Check that speed brakes
are retracted.
Smoothly recover the
flight path.
If in clean configuration and below  20000 :
Select FLAPS 1.
Flap Tab Fairing
One fairing may be partly or completely
missing.
When the forward
part of the fairing is missing, the aft part must be removed.
When the aft
part of the fairing is missing, the forward part may remain installed.
The following performance penalties
are applicable:
Takeoff and
approach climb performance limiting weights are reduced by  143  ( 315 )
En route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  158  ( 348 )
Fuel consumption
is increased by  0.32 .
Flap Tab Fairing
Two fairing may be partly or completely missing.
When the forward
part of the fairing is missing, the aft part must be removed.
When the aft
part of the fairing is missing, the forward part may remain installed.
The following performance penalties
are applicable per missing fairing:
Takeoff performance
limiting weight is reduced by  160  ( 353 )
Approach climb
limiting weight is reduced by  80  ( 176 )
En route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  207  ( 456 )
Fuel consumption
is increased by  0.32 .
Illustration Flap Tab Fairing
For dispatch conditions:
Approach Climb and Landing Climb
The approach climb speed is
at least 1.23  VS1G  of the approach configuration, approach
climb speed is permitted up to:
1.23  VS1G  in CONF 1+F, or
1.31  VS1G  in CONF 2, or CONF 3.
For approach and landing climb
limiting weight determination, the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO
approved FM modules at the latest approved revision must be used,  .
The
go around gradient conditions in landing configuration with two engines
operative in TOGA thrust mode are never limiting.
To take into account ice formation
on the non heated structure:
Decrease the
approach/landing climb limiting weight by  7.1 .
For landing
distance determination select ice accretion in the AFM_OCTO input
data Interface.
The all engines
go-around soft mode gradient in approach climb configuration is at
or above the approach climb gradient calculated by the Performance
Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module.
The go-around
soft mode gradient in landing climb configuration is the lower of  4  and the landing climb gradient calculated by the Performance Engineer's
Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM module.
LANDING DISTANCE DETERMINATION IN CASE OF IN-FLIGHT FAILURE
RUNWAY CONDITION DETERMINATION
The flight crew shall obtain the runway condition and/or the depth
and type of runway contaminant to make the basic assessment of actual
condition. Estimated Surface Friction (Mu) or Pilot Reports of Braking
Action (PiRep) or similar qualitative information may be used in addition.  Landing distance determination must not
consider a better Braking Action than the one related to the runway
condition.
Runway Condition
Max Reported Braking
Action
Dry
6 - DRY
Wet
5 - GOOD
Compacted Snow
4 - GOOD to MEDIUM
More than  3  of Dry or Wet Snow
3 - MEDIUM
More than  3  of Standing Water or Slush
2 - MEDIUM to POOR
Ice (Cold and Dry)
1 - POOR
LANDING DISTANCE DETERMINATION
The landing distance to be applied in
the case of failure is the Operational Landing Distance ( OLD ).  The  OLD  can be determined by selecting the failure case in the
IN-FLIGHT FAILURE field of the AFM_OCTO interface, using the database
given in the PERFORMANCE chapter of this manual ( ), combined with LSAE13.fail file using the
AFM_OCTO approved FM module at revision 36 or higher.

Electronic Flight Bag - Class 2
The dual Class 2 Electronic
Flight Bag ( EFB ) system is approved only as an installed  EFB  resources consisting of 2 docking stations and 2 display
units.  The definition of the Portable
Electronic Device ( PED ) hosted in the  EFB  docking
station and obtaining its approval is the responsibility of the operator.
The airworthiness approval is
based on the assumption that:
EFB  resources are used in accordance with  JAA
TGL  36 "Approval of Electronic Flight Bags ( EFB s)" or  FAA  Advisory Circular 120-76A and with Airbus document reference
00D460P7110/C01
the  PED  and its associated applications are developed and justified in accordance
with  JAA
TGL  36 "Approval of Electronic Flight
Bags ( EFB s)" or  FAA  Advisory Circular 120-76A
and with Airbus document reference 00D460P7110/C01.
Compliance with the above limitation
does not constitute an authorization to use the  EFB . Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.
Airborne Traffic Situational Awareness (ATSAW)
The Airborne Traffic Situational Awareness
( ATSAW ) function with Enhanced Traffic Situational Awareness
( ATSA-AIRB ), and Enhanced Visual Separation on Approach
( ATSA-VSA ), applications has been demonstrated to comply
with requirements of ED160/DO314.  This approval
is based on standards, descriptions, operational procedures and limitations
contained in " ATSAW  Aircraft capability declaration" document
certification reference 00D340P5238/C0S at the latest issue.
Compliance with
the above statement does not constitute an operation approval. Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.
The  ATSAW  function does not supersede air traffic control or navigation systems
and references.
The  ATSAW  function is an awareness tool only, the flight crew must refer to  TCAS  for collision avoidance purposes.

The  AFM  is approved in both
PDF and Ops Library Browser ( OLB ) formats.
OLB  version 5.1 or
higher must be used to consult the  AFM  in  OLB  format.

General
This Supplement is applicable
to aircraft fitted with Runway Overrun Prevention System ( ROPS ). In addition, the  TAWS - GPWS  Supplements remain
applicable for aircraft equipped with  ROPS .
Limitations
ROPS  is designed
to alert the flight crew in the case of potential runway overrun situation
for dry and wet runways only, and assumes both thrust reversers availability
and no failure affecting landing performance. The absence of  ROW  alert during approach must not affect flight crew's
judgment of conditions and reaction to other cues that would indicate
that a go around must be performed.  In the case of runways not validated for  ROPS  in the  GPWS  database,  ROPS  is inoperative. It is the operator’s
responsibility to inform the flight crews about the runways that are
not validated for  ROPS , along the scheduled route.  The  ROPS  may be inhibited ( TERR  set to OFF on  GPWS  panel) when the aircraft position is less than  15  from the airfield for specific approach procedures which have previously
been identified as potentially producing undue  ROW  alerts.  Operations where the  ROW  alert landing distance is greater than the  LDA  must not
be conducted unless a specific authorization is obtained from the
appropriate authority. For these runways,  ROW  alerts may
be triggered if  ROPS  is not inhibited (on the  GPWS  panel, the  TERR  is kept ON).  In the case of a failure that affects landing performance and/or
thrust reversers availability,  ROPS  does not prevent runway
excursion. Nevertheless, in the case of  ROPS  alert, the
flight crew must immediately apply  ROPS  procedures.
In the case of lateral
directional control difficulties at low speed in adverse conditions
(crosswind on the critical side, low runway friction) with a failure
leading to asymmetrical reverse thrust (thrust reverse failure or
dispatch with one thrust reverse inoperative), the flight crew should
regain directional control and then, follow SET/KEEP MAX REVERSE  ROP  alerts.
Limitations
ROPS  is designed
to alert the flight crew in the case of potential runway overrun situation
for dry and wet runways only, and assumes both thrust reversers availability
and no failure affecting landing performance. The absence of  ROW  alert during approach must not affect flight crew's judgment
of conditions and reaction to other cues that would indicate that
a go around must be performed.  In the case of runways not validated
for  ROPS  in the  TAWS  database,  ROPS  is inoperative. It is the operator’s responsibility to inform the
flight crews about the runways that are not validated for  ROPS , along the scheduled route.  The  ROPS  may be inhibited
( ROW/ROP  set to OFF on the  SURV  panel) for specific
approach procedures which have previously been identified as potentially
producing undue  ROW  alerts.  Operations where the  ROW  alert
landing distance is greater than the  ROPS  runway length
must not be conducted unless a specific authorization is obtained
from the appropriate authority. For these runways,  ROW  alerts
may be triggered if  ROPS  is not inhibited (on the  SURV  panel, the  ROW/ROP  is kept ON).
The  ROPS  runway length is in nominal case:
The  LDA  ( TAWS
T3CAS  configuration), or
The runway length
from Jeppesen source data ( TAWS
EGPWS  configuration)
In the case of a failure that affects landing performance and/or
thrust reversers availability,  ROPS  does not prevent runway
excursion. In the case of operations with performance credit on a
PFC/GROOVED runway,  ROPS  does not prevent runway excursion.Nevertheless, in the case of  ROPS  alert, the
flight crew must immediately apply  ROPS  procedures.
In the case of lateral
directional control difficulties at low speed in adverse conditions
(crosswind on the critical side, low runway friction) with a failure
leading to asymmetrical reverse thrust (thrust reverse failure or
dispatch with one thrust reverse inoperative), the flight crew should
regain directional control and then, follow SET/KEEP MAX REVERSE  ROP  alerts.
Abnormal Procedures
At landing, in the case of detected
runway overrun situation, the  ROPS  function triggers several
alerts.
If the amber message  IF WET: RWY TOO SHORT  is displayed on the  PFD :
If the runway is not dry:
Apply go around procedure
If the runway is dry:
The landing may be
continued
If the runway is wet and PFC or GROOVED and performance credit
for PFC/GROOVED runway is used:
The landing may be
continued
In this particular
situation, the flight crew must use all deceleration means.  The  IF WET: RWY TOO SHORT  alert is an indication
of marginal performance even on wet grooved/PFC runway.
If the red message  RWY TOO
SHORT  is displayed on the  PFD :
Apply go around procedure
When on ground:
If  MAX BRAKING  warning
is triggered:
Apply and keep maximum
brakes as long as necessary
Apply and keep max
reverse, keeping maximum brakes, as long as necessary
If  MAX REVERSE  warning
is triggered:
Apply and keep max
reverse
If  KEEP MAX REVERSE  warning
is triggered:
Maintain max reverse
as long as necessary. For the limitations associated with this aural
warning,
Abnormal Procedures
At landing, in the case of detected
runway overrun situation, the  ROPS  function triggers several
alerts.
If the amber message  RWY TOO SHORT  is displayed on the  PFD :
Consider go around
procedure
If the red message  RWY TOO
SHORT  is displayed on the  PFD :
Apply go around procedure
When on ground:
If  MAX BRAKING  warning
is triggered:
Apply and keep maximum
brakes as long as necessary
Apply and keep max
reverse, keeping maximum brakes, as long as necessary
If  MAX REVERSE  warning
is triggered:
Apply and keep max
reverse
If  KEEP MAX REVERSE  warning
is triggered:
Maintain max reverse
as long as necessary. For the limitations associated with this aural
warning,

Flight controls are in alternate
law.  .
Manually confirm emergency electrical
power on.

Start  APU  when below  250 .
Set all thrust levers to idle.
Gliding distance in no wind
condition:  2  /  1000 .
Gliding distance may
be adjusted by use of speed brakes.
Initiate the diversion.
For communications,
only  VHF  1 and  ATC  1 are available.
Notify  ATC .
Notify cabin crew.
Turn on cabin signs.
Use crew oxygen masks above  100 .

Set ENG MODE selector
to IGN START.
Attempt engines windmill
relight with optimum relight speed  260 .  .
Set all engine master
levers to OFF for  30  then ON.
Try regularly engines
relight.

Set all engine master
levers to OFF.
Optimum speed: green
dot.

Turn on  APU  bleed.

relight (only one at a time).

relight.

dot.

Ground Engine Operation in Icing Condition
In the following procedure
the flight crew must use visibility only (and not  RVR ).
When operating the engines on ground in icing conditions, with
the  OAT  at  +3  ( 37 ) or below:
If at any time during the operation of the engines on ground
there is freezing fog (FZFG) and the visibility is lower than  150  ( 500 ):
Takeoff is not authorized.
Request maintenance
action to deice the engine.
If there is FZFG and the visibility is equal or higher than  150  ( 500 ), or if there is no FZFG:
If the ground surface conditions and the environment permit:
Set the parking brake
to ON, or brake with pedals.
Accelerate the engines
to  60
N1  at intervals not greater than  30 . Maintain this thrust setting for  30 , or
for as long as possible up to  30 .
The engine acceleration
must be performed during both taxi-in and taxi-out.
The previous flight
taxi-in time exposure to icing conditions with the  OAT  at  +3  ( 37 ) or below must be considered when
establishing the interval for the first acceleration after engine
start.
This engine acceleration
must also be performed just before takeoff.
The takeoff
must occur not later than  5  after the last engine
acceleration for ice shedding
The last engine
acceleration for ice shedding may be performed together with the takeoff
procedure.
If during the thrust
increase the aircraft starts to move, immediately retard the thrust
levers to IDLE.
If the ground surface conditions or the environment do not
permit:
Takeoff is not authorized.
Request maintenance
action to deice the engine.

General
This supplement is applicable
to operations at LEH airport (VILH) .  This supplement does not constitute an operational approval. Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.  Unless amended in this supplement,
all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.
Limitations
At LEH airport, the mean runway slope is  2.9 .
Takeoff must be performed downhill.
The relative slope between approach slope and runway slope is limited
to less than  4.5 .
Landing must be performed uphill.
Autoland is not permitted.
Normal Procedures
Minimum approach speed is  VLS  +  5 .
The flare height should
be increased for landing at high altitude airports particularly with
high approach speeds or for approaches with increased glide slope
or to uphill sloping runways.
Performance
For takeoff and landing performance
determination, Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO approved FM
module at the latest approved revision must be used.  .
The mean runway slope
must be entered for performance determination.
Landing distance determination
does not require any speed increment.
Minimum approach climb gradient is  3 .
Minimum landing climb gradient is  4.1 .
ENG - HOT AIR LEAK
Turn off engine anti-ice of
the affected side.
Avoid icing conditions.
On ground:
Do not set affected
engine thrust lever above idle.
ENG - HOT AIR LEAK
Turn off engine anti-ice of
the affected side.
Avoid icing conditions.
On ground:
Set affected engine
thrust lever to idle.
ENG - STALL
Set affected engine thrust lever
to IDLE.
Check engine parameters of the
affected engine.
If abnormal engine parameters:
Shut down the affected
engine.
If normal engine parameters:
Turn on wing anti-ice.
Increase thrust slowly
on the affected engine.
If a stall recurs:
Reduce thrust on the
affected engine.
If a stall does not recur:
Continue engine operation.
ENG - STALL
Set affected engine thrust lever
to IDLE.
Check engine parameters of the
affected engine.
If abnormal engine parameters:
Shut down the affected
engine.
If normal engine parameters:
Turn on engine anti-ice
of the affected side.
Increase thrust slowly
on the affected engine.
If a stall recurs:
Reduce thrust on the
affected engine.
If a stall does not recur:
Continue engine operation.

Bullnose Seal
One per engine may be missing.
May be combined with
any other item listed in  MCDL -78 chapter.
The following performance penalties
are applicable:
Takeoff performance
limiting weight is reduced by  446  ( 983 ) per missing seal
Approach climb
limiting weight is reduced by  223  ( 492 ) per missing seal
En-route performance
limiting weight is reduced by  575  ( 1268 ) per missing seal
Fuel consumption
is increased by  0.81  per missing seal.

Establishment of Aircraft Noise Certificate
To establish the aircraft noise
certificate:
The weights
which must be taken into account for the determination of the certified
regulatory noise levels are determined by the appropriate authority
of the state of registration.
The certified
regulatory noise levels can be found in the noise certification documentation
(e.g.  EASA  Form 45 noise certificate) issued by the appropriate
authority of the state of registration. The APPENDICES AND SUPPLEMENTS
chapter of this  AFM  ( ) provides also data on the approved external noise levels.

Takeoff in Extreme Cold Weather
If  OAT  below  -40  ( -40 ):
Perform 5 thrust reversers cycles before
takeoff.
Takeoff has
to be initiated within one minute after the last thrust reversers
cycle.
If the takeoff
is not performed within one minute after the last thrust reversers
cycle, repeat each time the 5 thrust reversers cycles.
Takeoff in Extreme Cold Weather
If  OAT  below  -40  ( -40 ):
Perform 6 thrust reversers cycles before takeoff.
Takeoff has
to be initiated within one minute after the last thrust reversers
cycle.
If the takeoff
is not performed within one minute after the last thrust reversers
cycle, repeat each time the 6 thrust reversers cycles.

General
SATELLITE Antenna installation
This Airplane Flight Manual supplement
is for installation of a Satellite Antenna.  This Satellite Antenna is used by the
communications systems that provide passengers with access to internet
connection.  The installation
of this antenna leads to a slight drag increment.  Unless amended by this supplement, all
the  AFM  sections remain applicable.
Performance  The installation of KU Band Antenna or KA Band Antenna has
following impact on aircraft performance:
KU BAND ANTENNA PERFORMANCE IMPACT
The One Engine
Inoperative (OEI) net ceiling is reduced by  630 , which correspond
to  630 .
The Climb OEI
final take-off limiting weight penalty is  430 .
This weight penalty corresponds to a degradation of  0.05  of the final take-off gradient.
The other AFM performance model justifications remain valid considering
the installation of the antenna, following the low drag increment
induced by this modification.
KA BAND ANTENNA PERFORMANCE IMPACT
The One Engine
Inoperative (OEI) net ceiling is reduced by  610 , which correspond
to  610 .
The Climb OEI
final take-off limiting weight penalty is  470 .
This weight penalty corresponds to a degradation of  0.05  of the final take-off gradient.
The other AFM performance model justifications remain valid considering
the installation of the antenna, following the low drag increment
induced by this modification.

General
This supplement is published to enable
the intermix of several standards of combustors on aircraft equipped
with PW1100G-JM. This intermix is covered through Mod 163279.  On one engine, the Block B combustor
or the Block D combustor (Mod 167243) can be used instead of the Block
C3 combustor (Mod 160734) or Block C3+ combustor (Mod 165736).  Unless additional data is provided in
this  AFM  supplement, all the chapters of this  AFM  remain applicable.

Flight Operations Maintenance Exchanger ( FOMAX )
The Flight Operations MAintenance eXchanger
( FOMAX ) is an approved system designed to provide on-ground
and in-flight broadband connectivity with ground systems for maintenance
and flight operations purposes.
Its main functions are:
To provide a  WIFI  Access Point in the cockpit for Controlled Portable Electronic
Devices ( C-PED s) and,
To collect aircraft
maintenance and performance data, and automatically send them to ground-based
operations and to  EFB .
Controlled
Portable Electronic Devices ( C-PED s) can connect to  FOMAX . The definition of the  C-PED s, their hosted
software applications and their compliance with the applicable operational
regulations are under the responsibility of the operator.  When  FOMAX  is used by an  EFB , the operational
approval to be obtained by the operator (when applicable) is based
on the assumption that:
FOMAX  system is used
in accordance with Airbus document " FOMAX  Guidelines
and Limitations for  C-PED  on A320 and A330 Aircraft Families"
reference X46RP1800163 at the latest issue.
Compliance with the above limitation
does not constitute an authorization to use the  EFB . Such
authorization must be obtained by the operator from the appropriate
authorities.
ANTI ICE - CAPT + F/O (CAPT + STBY) (F/O + STBY) AOA HEAT FAULT
If non-related  ADR  on and operative:
Turn off one of affected  ADR s.
If non-related  ADR  faulty or off:
If icing conditions expected:
Turn off one of affected  ADR s.

characteristics:
a power output
less than 100mW and,
a frequency
band of 2.4GHz or/and 5 GHz.

to comply with airworthiness requirements contained in AMC 20-25 about
the use of the wireless transmitting capability of the  EFB  in the cockpit if the  EFB  portable resources comply with
the limitations stated above.  Compliance with the above does not constitute an operational approval.
Such authorization must be obtained by the operator from appropriate
authorities.

Certification CP336
Approval reference for DU belonging to Container CP336

Description of Contaminated Runway States
A runway is contaminated when
more than  25  of its surface is covered with:
Compacted snow  The maintenance personnel
use a snow groomer to compress the snow on a runway in order to obtain
a hard surface.
More than  3  ( 0.12 ) of dry snow  Dry snow is snow that,
if compacted by hand, does not stay compressed when released. The
wind can blow dry snow. The density of dry snow is approximately  0.2  ( 1.7 ).
More than  3  ( 0.12 ) of wet snow  Wet snow is snow that,
if compacted by hand, stays compressed when released, and with which
snowballs can be created. The density of wet snow is approximately  0.4  ( 3.35 ).
More than  3  ( 0.12 ) of standing water  Standing water occurs
due to heavy rain or not sufficient runway drainage.
More than  3  ( 0.12 ) of slush  Slush is snow soaked
with water, which spatters when stepped on firmly. Slush occurs at
temperatures of approximately  5 , and
has a density of approximately  0.85  ( 7.1 ).
Ice (cold and
dry)  Situation in which
ice occurs on the runway in cold and dry conditions.
Wet ice  When the ice on a
runway melts, or there are loose/fluid contaminants on top of the
ice, the ice is referred to as "wet ice". When there is
wet ice on a runway, braking and directional control are difficult
or not possible, because the runway surface is very slippery.

If flight conditions
permit, reduce affected engine thrust to minimise vibrations below
limit.

requirements contained in CS-ACNS Issue 3 Subpart E Section 3 (covering  AMC  1 CAT.GEN.MPA.210, AMC 1 & 2 CAT.IDE.A.280 and  AMC  1 CAT.IDE.A.285(f)) for the location of an aircraft in
distress.

FUEL - CTR L(R) XFR FAULT
If CTR TK XFR valve on the affected side is open:
Turn off the center
tank transfer valve of the affected side.
If unsuccessful:
Open crossfeed valve.
Turn off affected
side wing tank pumps.
When the center tank is empty:
Turn on affected side
wing tank pumps.
Close crossfeed valve.
If CTR TK XFR valve on the affected side is closed:
Set CTR TK MODE SEL
to MAN.
If unsuccessful:
Open crossfeed valve.
Turn off affected
side wing tank pumps.
When center tank is empty:
Turn on affected side
wing tank pumps.
Close crossfeed valve.
FUEL - L(R) WING TK OVERFLOW
If right wing tank overflow:
Close right side center
tank transfer valve.
If unsuccessful:
Open crossfeed valve.
Turn off right side
wing tank pumps.
When the center tank is empty:
Turn on right side
wing tank pumps.
Close crossfeed valve.
If left wing tank overflow:
Close left side center
tank transfer valve.
If overflow during  ACT  transfer:
Turn the ALL ACT pushbutton
to ISOL.
If unsuccessful:
Open crossfeed valve.
Turn off left side
wing tank pumps.
When the center tank is empty:
Turn on left side
wing tank pumps.
Close crossfeed valve.
FUEL - L(R) WING TK OVERFLOW
Close crossfeed valve.
Set the CTR TK MODE SEL to AUTO.
Turn off the affected side center
tank pump.
Expect fuel imbalance.
FUEL - L(R) WING TK OVERFLOW
Close center tank transfer valve
of the affected side.
If unsuccessful:
Open crossfeed valve.
Turn off the affected
side wing tank pumps.
When the center tank is empty:
Turn on affected side
wing tank pumps.
Close crossfeed valve.
FUEL - FQI CH 1+2 FAULT
Reset both  FQI s.
If unsuccessful:
Apply  FOB  display procedure.  .

AIR ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT and AIR ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT
This Temporary Revision ( TR ) provides the operational procedure that the flight crew must apply
in the case of an engine bleed overpressure:
identified by the "  AIR  ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT  " ECAM caution,
and combined with the associated engine bleed valve failed open, or
identified by the "  AIR  ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT  " ECAM caution.
All A320neo family aircraft with Mod 161561.
Do not remove this Temporary
Revision until instructed to do so.
EASA  2025-03-12T00:00:00  10086675
AIR ENG 1(2) BLEED FAULT
In case of NO overtemperature on the affected engine bleed
(temperature below  250 ):
If the affected engine bleed valve is failed open associated
with an overpressure (pressure above  60 ):
Turn off the affected
side engine bleed.
Turn off the wing
anti-ice.
If wing anti-ice is
shut off,  .
Open the crossbleed
valve.
Turn off the affected
side pack (if the opposite pack is available).
Turn off the  APU  bleed (if the engine 1 bleed is affected).
Avoid icing conditions.
Do not reset the affected engine bleed.
In all other cases:
Turn off the affected
side engine bleed.
Turn off the aft cargo
hot air (if installed).
If "  BLEED NOT CLOSED  " not displayed on  ECAM :
Turn off the affected
side pack (if the opposite pack is available).
Open the crossbleed
valve.
If "  BLEED NOT CLOSED  " displayed on  ECAM :
Turn off the affected
side pack.
Close the crossbleed
valve.
Turn off the wing
anti-ice.
If wing anti-ice is
shut off,  .
Turn off  APU  bleed (if the engine 1 bleed is affected).
In case of overtemperature on the affected engine bleed (temperature
above  250 ):
Turn off the affected
side engine bleed.
Turn off the aft cargo
hot air (if installed).
If "  BLEED NOT CLOSED  " not displayed on  ECAM :
Turn off the affected
side pack (if the opposite pack is available).
Open the crossbleed
valve.
If "  BLEED NOT CLOSED  " displayed on  ECAM :
Turn off the affected
side pack.
Close the crossbleed
valve.
Turn off the wing
anti-ice.
If wing anti-ice is
shut off,
Turn off  APU  bleed (if the engine 1 bleed is affected).
AIR ENG 1+2 BLEED FAULT
In case of NO overpressure on both engine bleeds (pressure
below  60  on both engine bleeds):
If NO LEAK:
Set the crossbleed
valve to auto.
Turn off wing anti-ice.
If wing anti-ice is shut off,  .
If  APU  bleed is on and fails:
Turn off  APU  bleed.
Turn off then on both
engine bleeds.
If no engine bleed reset successful:
Turn off both engine
bleeds.
Initiate a descent
to the higher of:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
Start  APU .
If  APU  available:
When below  200 :
Turn on  APU  bleed.
Turn off pack 2 if
pack 1 is operative.
If  APU  bleed available:
Maximum
flight level:  200 .
Turn on both packs.
Turn on both engine
bleeds.
Turn off  APU  bleed.
If no engine bleed reset successful:
Turn on  APU  bleed.
Turn off pack 2 if
pack 1 is operative.
Turn off both engine
bleeds.
If  APU  bleed not available:
Turn off  APU  bleed.
When at or below  100  or  MEA - MORA :
Turn on both packs.
Turn on both engine
bleeds.
If no engine bleed reset successful:
Turn off both engine
bleeds.
Maximum
flight level:  100  or  MEA - MORA .
When cabin differential pressure below  1 :
Turn on RAM AIR.

Turn off  APU  bleed.

bleeds.

bleeds.

Traffic Alert and
Collision Avoidance System (TCAS)
For normal  TCAS  operation,
select :
Transponder
: ON or AUTO
ALT report :
ON
TCAS  : TA/RA
Select  TA  for :
Dispatch with
landing gear down
Engine failure
Operation near
closely spaced parallel runway (less than  1200 )
Maneuvers must not be based
solely on information presented on the traffic display.
Compliance with a  TCAS  II Resolution Advisory ( RA ) is always required unless
the pilot considers it unsafe to do so. Compliance with a  RA  is required even if there is a conflict between the  RA  and an Air Traffic Control ( ATC ) instruction to maneuver.
Once an  RA  has been issued, safe separation could be compromised if current
vertical speed is changed, except as necessary to comply with the  RA . This is because  TCAS  II-to- TCAS  II
coordination may be in progress with the intruder aircraft, and any
change in vertical speed that does not comply with the  RA  may negate the effectiveness of the other aircraft's compliance
with the  RA .
If a  RA  "Climb" or "Increase Climb"
is triggered when the aircraft is in approach in CONF3 or FULL:
Perform a go around.
Following a  TCAS  II
"clear of conflict" advisory, the pilot should expeditiously
return to the applicable  ATC  clearance unless otherwise
directed by  ATC .
Traffic Alert and
Collision Avoidance System (TCAS)
For normal  TCAS  operation,
set:
Transponder
ON or AUTO
ALT report
ON
TCAS
TA/RA
Select  TA  for:
Dispatch with
landing gear down
Engine failure
Operation near
closely spaced parallel runway (less than  1200 )
Maneuvers must not be based
solely on information presented on the traffic display.
Compliance with a  TCAS  II Resolution Advisory ( RA ) is always required unless
the pilot considers it unsafe to do so. Compliance with a  RA  is required even if there is a conflict between the  RA  and an Air Traffic Control ( ATC ) instruction to maneuver.
Once an  RA  has been issued, safe separation could be compromised if current
vertical speed is changed, except as necessary to comply with the  RA . This is because  TCAS  II-to- TCAS  II
coordination may be in progress with the intruder aircraft, and any
change in vertical speed that does not comply with the  RA  may negate the effectiveness of the other aircraft's compliance
with the  RA .
If a  RA  "Climb" or "Increase Climb"
is triggered when the aircraft is in approach in CONF3 or FULL:
Perform a go around.
If AP/FD TCAS mode available:
If for any reason
during an  RA , the aircraft vertical speed does not reach
the green area of the vertical speed scale, the pilot flying should
disconnect the  AP , and override the  FD  orders,
in order to lead the aircraft vertical speed out of the red area of
the vertical speed scale.
Following a  TCAS  II
"clear of conflict" advisory, the pilot should expeditiously
return to the applicable  ATC  clearance unless otherwise
directed by  ATC .
Terrain Awareness
and Warning System - Enhanced Ground Proximity Warning System (TAWS-EGPWS)
When a warning occurs:
Pull up using full
back stick.
Apply takeoff thrust
and climb until the warning ceases.
For enhanced function, in addition to climbing, a turning maneuver
can be initiated after verifying the aircraft position and if the
crew concludes turning is the safest way of action.
The  EGPWS  does not
take account of specific aircraft configuration and climb performance
and a "pull up" maneuver on its own, for certain situations,
may not ensure terrain clearance.
Warnings may be considered cautionary during daylight  VMC  conditions provided the cause of the warning can be identified immediately.
When a caution occurs:
Adjust the flight
path/configuration so that the caution alert ceases.
Climb and/or turn
as necessary based on analysis of all available instruments and information.
Terrain Awareness
and Warning System - Enhanced Ground Proximity Warning System (TAWS-EGPWS)
When a warning occurs:
Pull up using full
back stick.
Apply takeoff thrust
and climb until the warning ceases.
For enhanced function, in addition to climbing, a turning maneuver
can be initiated after verifying the aircraft position and if the
crew concludes turning is the safest way of action.
The  EGPWS  does not
take account of specific aircraft configuration and climb performance
and a "pull up" maneuver on its own, for certain situations,
may not ensure terrain clearance.
Warnings may be considered cautionary during daylight  VMC  conditions provided the cause of the warning can be identified immediately.
When a caution occurs:
Adjust the flight
path/configuration so that the caution alert ceases.
Climb and/or turn
as necessary based on analysis of all available instruments and information.
During climb, descent, approach and go-around, when the  FMS  navigation accuracy check prevents the crew from using NAV mode
in a phase of flight, the  EGPWS  enhanced function must
be switched OFF through the TERR pushbutton located on the  GPWS  panel.
Terrain Awareness
and Warning System - Enhanced Ground Proximity Warning System (TAWS-EGPWS)
When a warning occurs:
Pull up using full
back stick.
Apply takeoff thrust
and climb until the warning ceases.
For  TAWS  predictive function, in addition to climbing,
a turning maneuver can be initiated after verifying the aircraft position
and if the crew concludes turning is the safest way of action.
ln the case of an "Avoid
Terrain" alert, in addition to climbing, a turning maneuver is
recommended.
Warnings may be considered cautionary during daylight  VMC  conditions provided the cause of the warning can be identified immediately.
When a caution occurs:
Adjust the flight
path/configuration so that the caution alert ceases.
Climb and/or turn
as necessary based on analysis of all available instruments and information.
Runway Overrun Warning
- Runway Overrun Protection (ROW-ROP)
The flight crew must not begin
an approach if the  ROW  Distance is greater than the Landing
Distance Available.
The means to assess the  ROW  landing distance is to select the “Operational Landing
Distance" calculated by the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO
approved FM module using:
The database
given in PERFORMANCE chapter ( ) of this  AFM ,
The landing
fail file given in EMER-GEN chapter ( ) and in ABN-GEN chapter ( ) of this  AFM .
Runway Overrun Warning
- Runway Overrun Protection (ROW-ROP)
The flight crew must not begin
an approach if the  ROW  Distance is greater than the  ROPS  runway length.
The  ROPS  runway length
is in nominal case:
The  LDA  ( TAWS
T3CAS  configuration), or
The runway length
from Jeppesen source data ( TAWS
EGPWS  configuration)
Set the appropriate runway condition (WET/DRY).
The means to assess the  ROW  landing distance is to select the “Operational Landing
Distance" calculated by the Performance Engineer's Programs/AFM_OCTO
approved FM module using:
The database
given in PERFORMANCE chapter ( ) of this  AFM ,
The landing
fail file given in EMER-GEN chapter ( ) and in ABN-GEN chapter ( ) of this  AFM .
Automatic landing and Rollout with Outdated MagVar
Automatic landing and rollout operations
are not permitted when the difference between the magnetic variation
at a given airport, and the magnetic variation of the  ADIRU  installed on the aircraft is more than  3 .
The magnetic variation
to be taken into account for a given airport is the most recent value
provided by the local Aeronautical Information Publication (AIP).
CAT II approaches without AUTOLAND
are still allowed.
